# Floating-point algebraic laws in a Lean toy model: reassociation, contraction, signed zero and NaN

## Summary

This package is a self-contained, Mathlib-free Lean 4 development of a **toy model** of IEEE 754 binary floating-point arithmetic (roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode, gradual underflow, both signed zeros, ±∞, and a single NaN whose payload and sign are not represented) and of LLVM-style fast-math rewrite flags (`nnan`, `ninf`, `nsz` as restrictions; `reassoc`, `contract` as permissions to evaluate a reassociated or contracted form generated by `Expr.variants`). It answers which algebraic laws hold for this arithmetic, which special values break each failing law, which flag sets license each rewrite, what contraction into a fused multiply-add gains and loses, and when evaluating one operation in a wider format and rounding back equals evaluating it directly.

The package contains 23 kernel-checked results: 8 theorems, 8 characterizations (statements with an `↔`), 6 counterexample results and 1 impossibility result. Every general statement is proved for every format with exponent-field width `e ≥ 2` and precision `p ≥ 2`; results stated for a named concrete format (their Lean names end in `_F33` or another format suffix) are about that format, and the prose does not generalize them. 9 hypotheses are marked `tight`, each with a named, kernel-checked counterexample in which all other hypotheses hold and the conclusion fails; the class of each counterexample is recorded in its ledger, and `tight` means no more than that the hypothesis cannot be dropped within that class. The remaining 23 ledger entries are the format invariants `2 ≤ e`, `2 ≤ p` (fields of `Fmt`), marked `unknown`. Every result has a kernel-checked non-vacuity witness, and the impossibility result has a positive control. The sentences below are the plain-language claims recorded in `results.json`, verbatim.

1. **`add_mul_fma_comm`** (theorem, T0). In every format of the toy model (one NaN, payload and sign not represented), for all values x, y, z including NaN, ±∞ and ±0: add x y = add y x, mul x y = mul y x, and fma x y z = fma y x z (the two multiplicands of fma swapped), as identity of encodings.
2. **`add_negZero_mul_one_identity`** (theorem, T0). In every format of the toy model, for every value x (including NaN, ±∞ and both zeros): x + (−0) = x and x * 1 = x, as identity of encodings.
3. **`add_posZero_identity_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x + (+0) = x holds if and only if x ≠ −0 (at x = −0 the sum is +0), and x + (+0) equals x up to the sign of zero (EqNsz) for every x.
4. **`sub_self_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x − x = +0 if and only if x is finite (this includes x = ±0), and x − x is NaN if and only if x is not finite (x is NaN, +∞ or −∞).
5. **`feq_self_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for every value x: the IEEE quiet equality x == x (feq) is true if and only if x is not NaN.
6. **`mul_posZero_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x * (+0) = +0 if and only if x is finite and its sign bit is clear; if x is not finite (NaN, +∞ or −∞), x * (+0) is NaN; and if x is finite with the sign bit set (−0 and every negative finite value), x * (+0) is −0.
7. **`add_assoc_fails_by_rounding`** (counterexample, T0). For every format with p + 1 ≤ 2·bias, the exposed witnesses a = 2^(emin+p) and b = 2^emin (emin = 1 − bias; assocRoundA, assocRoundB) satisfy all of the following: a, b, a + b, b + b, (a + b) + b and a + (b + b) are normal; (a + b) + b = a; and a + (b + b) ≠ a. So rounding alone breaks associativity of add, with no special values, underflow or overflow involved.
8. **`add_assoc_fails_by_overflow`** (counterexample, T0). In every format of the toy model, with M the largest finite value: (M + M) + (−M) = +∞ while M + (M + (−M)) = M.
9. **`add_assoc_zero_sign_impossible`** (impossibility, T1). In every format of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, gradual underflow), for all a, b, c: if (a + b) + c and a + (b + c) are equal up to the sign of zero, then they are identical. So under round-to-nearest-even with gradual underflow the two bracketings of a sum of three values never differ only in the sign of zero.
10. **`add_assoc_survives_restriction_flags`** (counterexample, T0). In format (3,3), at the exposed input (x, y, z) = (2, 1/4, 1/4) (envAssocRound): the source (x + y) + z is admissible under the flag set {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract} (no NaN or infinite argument or result), evaluates to 2, the target x + (y + z) evaluates to 2.5, and the target is not related by Rel {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract} to the value of any form of the source generated by Expr.variants {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract}; hence (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) is not ValidMod {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract}. So in this model reassoc is not implied by the other flags. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).
11. **`permissions_refine_iff_laws`** (characterization, T0). (Pointwise characterization.) In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: every form of (x + y) + z generated by Expr.variants {reassoc} evaluates at (a, b, c) to the value of (x + y) + z if and only if (a + b) + c = a + (b + c); and every form of x * y + z generated by Expr.variants {contract} evaluates at (a, b, c) to the value of x * y + z if and only if fma a b c = a * b + c. (Witnesses in every format.) With M the largest finite value: (M + M) + (−M) = +∞ while its permitted form M + (M + (−M)) = M, and M * 2 + (−M) = +∞ while its permitted form fma M 2 (−M) = M; so at these exposed inputs not every permitted form agrees with the source, in every format.
12. **`fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal`** (counterexample, T0). In format (3,3), for a = 5/16, b = 7/8, c = 2: the exact product a·b is 70 units of 2^−8 (35/128, at least the smallest normal 1/4, so no underflow), the rounded product a * b is 1/4, the inputs, the rounded product and both results are normal, fma a b c = 2.5, and a * b + c = 2. So contraction changes results on finite inputs by rounding alone.
13. **`fma_nearest`** (theorem, T0). In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c and every value r: if fma a b c is finite, then its absolute error with respect to the exact a·b + c is at most the error of r (the error of r is infinite when r is NaN or ±∞). So a finite fused result is a nearest value of the format to the exact a·b + c.
14. **`fma_errLe_twoStep`** (theorem, T1). In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: if fma a b c is finite, its error with respect to the exact a·b + c is at most the error of the two-step a * b + c (infinite if the two-step result overflows). Finiteness of the two-step result is not a sufficient hypothesis: in format (3,3), for (3/4, 12, 6) the two-step result is 14 while the fused result overflows to +∞, and the fused error is not at most the two-step error.
15. **`contraction_hazard_mulSubMul`** (counterexample, T0). In format (3,3), at a = 3/16, b = 3: the product a * b = 9/16 rounds to 1/2, the flag-free value of a*b − a*b is +0, and Expr.variants {contract} generates the form fma a b (−(a*b)) of a*b − a*b, whose value is 1/16 (the rounding error of the product). So contraction is not semantics-preserving even for an expression whose real value is 0.
16. **`double_rounding_not_innocuous`** (counterexample, T0). With narrow format (3,3) (p = 3) and wide format (3,4) (p' = 4): 3/16 + 2.5 computed by addVia (convert both operands to the wide format, add there, convert back, each step correctly rounded) is 3, while direct addition in the narrow format gives 2.5.
17. **`double_rounding_add_innocuous`** (theorem, T0). For all formats f (narrow, precision p) and g (wide, precision p'), if 2p + 1 ≤ p' and bias f ≤ bias g, then for all values x, y of f: addVia g x y (convert x and y to g, add in g, convert the sum back to f, each step correctly rounded with roundTiesToEven) equals add x y.
18. **`double_rounding_mul_innocuous`** (theorem, T0). For all formats f (narrow, precision p) and g (wide, precision p'), if 2p ≤ p', 2·qneg f ≤ qneg g (the smallest subnormal of g is at most the square of that of f) and bias f ≤ bias g, then for all values x, y of f: mulVia g x y (convert x and y to g, multiply in g, convert the product back to f, each step correctly rounded with roundTiesToEven) equals mul x y.
19. **`lt_not_ge_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b: the IEEE comparison a < b (lt) equals ¬(a >= b) if and only if neither a nor b is NaN.
20. **`minLt_maxLt_commutativity_characterization`** (characterization, T0). In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b: minLt a b = minLt b a (with minLt a b = if a < b then a else b) holds if and only if it is not the case that exactly one of a, b is NaN or that {a, b} = {+0, −0}; the same holds for maxLt a b = if b < a then a else b.
21. **`minimum_maximum_comm_assoc`** (theorem, T0). In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: the operations minimum and maximum modelled on IEEE 754-2019 §9.6 (NaN-propagating, with −0 below +0) are commutative and associative as identity of encodings.
22. **`flag_table_sufficiency`** (theorem, T1). In every format of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, one collapsed NaN), with ValidMod quantifying over the forms generated by Expr.variants: x + (−0) → x, x * 1 → x, x * (−1) → −x, x * 2 → x + x, (−0) − x → −x and x + y → y + x are valid without flags; x + (+0) → x, (+0) − x → −x and −(x − y) → y − x are ValidMod {nsz}; x − x → +0 is ValidMod {nnan}; x * (+0) → +0 is ValidMod {nnan, nsz}; (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) and (x * y) * z → x * (y * z) are ValidMod {reassoc}; x * y + z → fma x y z is ValidMod {contract}. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).
23. **`flag_table_exact_F33`** (characterization, T1). In format (3,3) of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, one collapsed NaN), with ValidMod quantifying over the forms generated by Expr.variants, for every flag set T: x + (−0) → x, x * 1 → x, x * (−1) → −x, x * 2 → x + x, (−0) − x → −x and x + y → y + x are ValidMod T; x + (+0) → x, (+0) − x → −x and −(x − y) → y − x are ValidMod T iff nsz ∈ T; x − x → +0 iff nnan ∈ T; x * (+0) → +0 iff nnan ∈ T and nsz ∈ T; (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) and (x * y) * z → x * (y * z) iff reassoc ∈ T; x * y + z → fma x y z iff contract ∈ T; and fma x y z → x * y + z is ValidMod T for no T. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).

*An agent relying on a result from this package must quote its plain-language claim and the relevant hypotheses and non-claims, not just its name or a paraphrase.*

Model–system relation: none proved. These are statements about the toy model; they neither establish nor refute the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean `v4.34.1`'s built-in `Float`, `Float32` or `Init.Data.Float.Model`. Every connection to a real system is a separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claim under **Findings → Modelling bridges**. Every result has **non-claims**, listed first under **Boundaries**; read them before relying on a result.

## Applicability

### Model fidelity

All results are about the toy model `Toy.Float`, defined in `lean/Toy/Float/Defs.lean` and quoted under **Findings → Model definitions**. It is meant to capture IEEE 754 binary floating-point arithmetic with the rounding-direction attribute roundTiesToEven, in every binary format with exponent-field width `e ≥ 2` and precision `p ≥ 2` (binary32 is `e = 8`, `p = 24`), together with a simplified model of LLVM-style fast-math flags on expressions built from variables, literals, `neg`, `add`, `sub`, `mul` and `fma`.

What the model contains: values given by their IEEE fields (sign, biased exponent, trailing significand), both signed zeros, subnormals with gradual underflow, `±∞`, and one NaN; exact arithmetic on integers of units (one unit is the smallest positive subnormal `2^-qneg`); correctly rounded `add`, `sub`, `mul` and `fma` with the IEEE 754 §6.3 sign rules for zero results and overflow to `±∞` at the roundTiesToEven threshold; the IEEE quiet comparisons; `minimum` and `maximum`; `min`/`max` written with `<`; correctly rounded conversion between formats; and the flag model `Flags`, `Admissible`, `Rel`, `Expr.variants`, `ValidMod`.

Known gaps (each is a difference from IEEE 754 or from LLVM, C or Rust, not a result):

- One NaN: payloads and the NaN sign are collapsed, so `=` on `FP f` is equality of the IEEE fields (identity of encodings, in the sense fixed under **Findings → Model definitions**) modulo NaN payload and NaN sign. Real hardware and LLVM choose NaN payloads and signs non-deterministically.
- No signaling NaNs, exception status flags or traps; roundTiesToEven is the sole rounding direction; no flush-to-zero.
- No division, square root, remainder, or conversions to and from integers.
- `reassoc` and `contract` are permissions with a one-step-per-node reading through `Expr.variants`, narrower than LLVM's `reassoc` ("algebraically equivalent transformations"). `ValidMod` is not transitive (`reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33`).
- `nsz` is modelled as a relation on results (`EqNsz`, equality up to the sign of zero), not as LLVM's current wording, which flips the sign of zero inputs; the two are compared under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.
- `nnan`/`ninf` exclude inputs on which some argument or result of a node of the source expression is NaN or infinite; this mirrors LLVM poison semantics when every instruction of the source carries the flag.

Model–system relation: none proved. These are statements about the toy model; they neither establish nor refute the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean `v4.34.1`'s built-in `Float`, `Float32` or `Init.Data.Float.Model`, and no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

### Relationship of the subjects

The Lean toolchain listed in `REPORT.json` is the checker of every theorem. The other subjects (the IEEE 754 standard, Goldberg's survey, Figueroa's and Roux's double-rounding results, the LLVM source revision and the Rust and C texts named under **Evidence**) are the specifications and prior art against which the definitions and the modelling bridges were compared. None of them is formally connected to the model.

### Lean version and project

- Toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1` (tag commit `5045d0056413266e57c625dcd7c365b10e377c52`), the core library without Mathlib and without Lake dependencies (`lean/lakefile.toml` declares one library, `Toy`, and no `[[require]]`).
- Namespace `Toy.Float`. The general statements are in `lean/Toy/Float/Statements.lean`; their proofs use `lean/Toy/Float/Arith.lean`, `Props.lean` and `Rounding.lean`. Counterexamples, non-vacuity witnesses and positive controls are in `Counterexamples.lean`, `Examples.lean` and `Table.lean`; `Bounded.lean` holds exhaustive kernel evaluations (`decide +kernel`, no `native_decide`) in small formats that independently cross-check three of the general theorems.
- A clean uncached `lake build` takes about two minutes and peaks at about 1.2 GB resident memory, almost all of it in `Bounded.lean`.

## Findings

Every Lean block below quotes the source verbatim with its `path:line` range relative to `lean/`; proofs are elided (`:= by …` or `:= …`). `Unfolded (prose)` paragraphs explain a definition in words and are not Lean terms.

### Model definitions

A **format** `f : Fmt` has exponent-field width `e ≥ 2` and precision `p ≥ 2` (significand bits including the hidden bit), as in an IEEE 754 binary interchange format (binary32: `e = 8`, `p = 24`). A **value** `FP f` is given by its IEEE fields: sign, biased exponent `E` (`E = 0`: zero or subnormal), trailing significand `M`, plus `±∞` and **one** NaN (payload and NaN sign are collapsed). Equality on `FP f` is Lean's structural equality on this inductive type: same constructor and same arguments, that is, the same IEEE fields (sign, biased exponent, trailing significand; the sign for `±∞`; nothing for the single NaN). In this report *identity of encodings*, and every `=` between values of `FP f`, means this field equality, i.e. equality of the IEEE fields modulo NaN payload and NaN sign. The IEEE bit pattern `FP.bits` is a separate function: its injectivity, and hence the agreement of field equality with equality of bit patterns, is proved in format (3,3) by `ex_bits_injective_F33`; injectivity of `FP.bits` in other formats, including a general format `f`, is not proved here.

Exact semantics. A finite value is an integer number of **units** `2^-qneg` (the smallest positive subnormal), `qneg = bias + p - 2`. Exact sums are integers of units, and exact products and exact `a*b + c` are integers of `2^-2qneg`. `round f neg m d` is round-to-nearest-ties-to-even (RNE) of `±m/2^d` units. It overflows to `±∞` when the RNE value with unbounded exponent exceeds the largest finite value, which is IEEE's `≥ 2^emax(2 − 2^-p)` rule.

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:38-42
structure Fmt where
  e : Nat
  p : Nat
  two_le_e : 2 ≤ e
  two_le_p : 2 ≤ p
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:81-85
inductive FP (f : Fmt) where
  | nan : FP f
  | inf (neg : Bool) : FP f
  | fin (neg : Bool) (E : Fin f.expCount) (M : Fin f.fracCount) : FP f
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:91-92
def unitsOf (f : Fmt) (E M : Nat) : Nat :=
  if E = 0 then M else (f.fracCount + M) * 2 ^ (E - 1)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:165-168
def rneDiv (m d : Nat) : Nat :=
  let q := m / 2 ^ d
  let r := m % 2 ^ d
  if 2 * r > 2 ^ d ∨ (2 * r = 2 ^ d ∧ q % 2 = 1) then q + 1 else q
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:174-176
def roundUnits (f : Fmt) (m d : Nat) : Nat :=
  let t := bitLen (m / 2 ^ d) - f.p
  rneDiv m (d + t) * 2 ^ t
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:189-192
def mkFin (f : Fmt) (neg : Bool) (n : Nat) : FP f :=
  if f.maxUnits < n then .inf neg
  else .fin neg ⟨decodeE f n % f.expCount, Nat.mod_lt _ f.expCount_pos⟩
                ⟨decodeM f n % f.fracCount, Nat.mod_lt _ f.fracCount_pos⟩
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:196-197
def round (f : Fmt) (neg : Bool) (m d : Nat) : FP f :=
  mkFin f neg (roundUnits f m d)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:217-226
def add : FP f → FP f → FP f
  | .nan, _ => .nan
  | .inf _, .nan => .nan
  | .fin .., .nan => .nan
  | .inf s, .inf t => if s = t then .inf s else .nan
  | .inf s, .fin .. => .inf s
  | .fin .., .inf t => .inf t
  | .fin s E M, .fin t E' M' =>
    let v := sval s (unitsOf f E.val M.val) + sval t (unitsOf f E'.val M'.val)
    if v = 0 then zero f (s && t) else round f (decide (v < 0)) v.natAbs 0
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:229
def sub (x y : FP f) : FP f := add x y.neg
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:234-242
def mul : FP f → FP f → FP f
  | .nan, _ => .nan
  | .inf _, .nan => .nan
  | .fin .., .nan => .nan
  | .inf s, .inf t => .inf (s != t)
  | .inf s, .fin t E M => if unitsOf f E.val M.val = 0 then .nan else .inf (s != t)
  | .fin s E M, .inf t => if unitsOf f E.val M.val = 0 then .nan else .inf (s != t)
  | .fin s E M, .fin t E' M' =>
    round f (s != t) (unitsOf f E.val M.val * unitsOf f E'.val M'.val) f.qneg
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:246-248
def exactFma (f : Fmt) (s : Bool) (ua : Nat) (t : Bool) (ub : Nat) (u : Bool) (uc : Nat) :
    Int :=
  sval (s != t) (ua * ub) + sval u (uc * 2 ^ f.qneg)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:255-274
def fma (a b c : FP f) : FP f :=
  match a, b with
  | .nan, _ => .nan
  | .inf _, .nan => .nan
  | .fin .., .nan => .nan
  | .fin s E M, .fin t E' M' =>
    match c with
    | .nan => .nan
    | .inf u => .inf u
    | .fin u E'' M'' =>
      let v := exactFma f s (unitsOf f E.val M.val) t (unitsOf f E'.val M'.val)
        u (unitsOf f E''.val M''.val)
      if v = 0 then zero f ((s != t) && u) else round f (decide (v < 0)) v.natAbs f.qneg
  | a, b =>
    if a.isZero || b.isZero then .nan
    else
      match c with
      | .nan => .nan
      | .inf u => if u = (a.signBit != b.signBit) then .inf u else .nan
      | .fin .. => .inf (a.signBit != b.signBit)
```

`add` implements IEEE 754 §6.3 for RNE: an exact zero sum is `+0` unless both operands are `-0` (`zero f (s && t)`). `mul` keeps the XOR sign also on underflow to zero. `fma` rounds the exact `a*b + c` once. An exact zero result is signed by the sum rule; a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero keeps its sign. Examples: `ex_add_pos_F33`, `ex_add_neg_F33`, `ex_mul_pos_F33`, `ex_fma_pos_F33`, `ex_round_pos_F33` (`2.25 → 2`, `2.75 → 3`, `15 → +∞`, `14.5 → 14` in (3,3)).

Comparisons and min/max:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:307-310
def key : FP f → Int
  | .nan => 0
  | .inf s => sval s (f.maxUnits + 1)
  | .fin s E M => sval s (unitsOf f E.val M.val)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:313
def lt (x y : FP f) : Bool := !x.isNaN && !y.isNaN && decide (key x < key y)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:315
def le (x y : FP f) : Bool := !x.isNaN && !y.isNaN && decide (key x ≤ key y)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:317
def ge (x y : FP f) : Bool := le y x
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:319
def feq (x y : FP f) : Bool := !x.isNaN && !y.isNaN && decide (key x = key y)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:322
def minLt (x y : FP f) : FP f := if lt x y then x else y
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:324
def maxLt (x y : FP f) : FP f := if lt y x then x else y
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:327
def totalKey (x : FP f) : Int := 2 * key x + (if x = posZero f then 1 else 0)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:331-332
def minimum (x y : FP f) : FP f :=
  if x.isNaN || y.isNaN then .nan else if totalKey x ≤ totalKey y then x else y
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:335-336
def maximum (x y : FP f) : FP f :=
  if x.isNaN || y.isNaN then .nan else if totalKey y ≤ totalKey x then x else y
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:339
def zeroBlind (x : FP f) : FP f := if x.isZero then posZero f else x
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:342
def EqNsz (x y : FP f) : Prop := zeroBlind x = zeroBlind y
```

Errors of approximations to `a*b + c` (infinite error = `none`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:281
def FP.val2 (x : FP f) : Int := sval x.signBit (x.units * 2 ^ f.qneg)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:285-286
def exactMulAdd (a b c : FP f) : Int :=
  exactFma f a.signBit a.units b.signBit b.units c.signBit c.units
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:291-292
def errOf (x : FP f) (v : Int) : Option Nat :=
  if x.isFinite then some (x.val2 - v).natAbs else none
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:295-298
def ErrLe : Option Nat → Option Nat → Prop
  | _, none => True
  | none, some _ => False
  | some a, some b => a ≤ b
```

Conversion and evaluation in a wider format:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:349-355
def cvt (g f : Fmt) : FP g → FP f
  | .nan => .nan
  | .inf s => .inf s
  | .fin s E M =>
    let u := unitsOf g E.val M.val
    if g.qneg ≤ f.qneg then round f s (u * 2 ^ (f.qneg - g.qneg)) 0
    else round f s u (g.qneg - f.qneg)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:358
def addVia (g : Fmt) (x y : FP f) : FP f := cvt g f (add (cvt f g x) (cvt f g y))
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:360
def mulVia (g : Fmt) (x y : FP f) : FP f := cvt g f (mul (cvt f g x) (cvt f g y))
```

#### Rewrites and flags

A rewrite `lhs → rhs` is a pair of expressions over `n` variables. `nnan`/`ninf` restrict the inputs: no argument **or result** of any node of `lhs` is NaN/infinite, as in LLVM's poison semantics when every instruction carries the flag. `nsz` coarsens the result relation to equality up to the sign of zero. `reassoc`/`contract` are **permissions**: the compiler may evaluate any *permitted form* of `lhs`.

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:368-374
structure Flags where
  nnan : Bool := false
  ninf : Bool := false
  nsz : Bool := false
  reassoc : Bool := false
  contract : Bool := false
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:389-397
inductive Expr (f : Fmt) (n : Nat) where
  | var (i : Fin n)
  | lit (c : FP f)
  | neg (a : Expr f n)
  | add (a b : Expr f n)
  | sub (a b : Expr f n)
  | mul (a b : Expr f n)
  | fma (a b c : Expr f n)
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:404-411
def eval (env : Fin n → FP f) : Expr f n → FP f
  | .var i => env i
  | .lit c => c
  | .neg a => (a.eval env).neg
  | .add a b => Toy.Float.add (a.eval env) (b.eval env)
  | .sub a b => Toy.Float.sub (a.eval env) (b.eval env)
  | .mul a b => Toy.Float.mul (a.eval env) (b.eval env)
  | .fma a b c => Toy.Float.fma (a.eval env) (b.eval env) (c.eval env)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:415-423
def nodeVals (env : Fin n → FP f) : Expr f n → List (FP f)
  | .var i => [env i]
  | .lit c => [c]
  | .neg a => (Expr.neg a).eval env :: a.nodeVals env
  | .add a b => (Expr.add a b).eval env :: (a.nodeVals env ++ b.nodeVals env)
  | .sub a b => (Expr.sub a b).eval env :: (a.nodeVals env ++ b.nodeVals env)
  | .mul a b => (Expr.mul a b).eval env :: (a.nodeVals env ++ b.nodeVals env)
  | .fma a b c => (Expr.fma a b c).eval env ::
      (a.nodeVals env ++ b.nodeVals env ++ c.nodeVals env)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:426-428
def reassocAdd (a b : Expr f n) : List (Expr f n) :=
  (match a with | .add x y => [Expr.add x (Expr.add y b)] | _ => []) ++
  (match b with | .add y z => [Expr.add (Expr.add a y) z] | _ => [])
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:437-439
def contractAdd (a b : Expr f n) : List (Expr f n) :=
  (match a with | .mul x y => [Expr.fma x y b] | _ => []) ++
  (match b with | .mul x y => [Expr.fma x y a] | _ => [])
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:443-445
def contractSub (a b : Expr f n) : List (Expr f n) :=
  (match a with | .mul x y => [Expr.fma x y (Expr.neg b)] | _ => []) ++
  (match b with | .mul x y => [Expr.fma (Expr.neg x) y a] | _ => [])
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:452-464
def variants (S : Flags) : Expr f n → List (Expr f n)
  | .var i => [.var i]
  | .lit c => [.lit c]
  | .neg a => (a.variants S).map .neg
  | .add a b => (a.variants S).flatMap fun a' => (b.variants S).flatMap fun b' =>
      Expr.add a' b' :: ((if S.reassoc then reassocAdd a' b' else []) ++
        (if S.contract then contractAdd a' b' else []))
  | .sub a b => (a.variants S).flatMap fun a' => (b.variants S).flatMap fun b' =>
      Expr.sub a' b' :: (if S.contract then contractSub a' b' else [])
  | .mul a b => (a.variants S).flatMap fun a' => (b.variants S).flatMap fun b' =>
      Expr.mul a' b' :: (if S.reassoc then reassocMul a' b' else [])
  | .fma a b c => (a.variants S).flatMap fun a' => (b.variants S).flatMap fun b' =>
      (c.variants S).map fun c' => Expr.fma a' b' c'
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:473-474
def Admissible (S : Flags) (e : Expr f n) (env : Fin n → FP f) : Prop :=
  ∀ v ∈ e.nodeVals env, (S.nnan = true → v.isNaN = false) ∧ (S.ninf = true → v.isInf = false)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:481
def Rel (S : Flags) (x y : FP f) : Prop := if S.nsz then EqNsz x y else x = y
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:494-496
def ValidMod (S : Flags) (r : Rewrite f n) : Prop :=
  ∀ env : Fin n → FP f, Admissible S r.lhs env →
    ∃ v ∈ r.lhs.variants S, Rel S (r.rhs.eval env) (v.eval env)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:500
def Valid (r : Rewrite f n) : Prop := ValidMod {} r
```

Unfolded (prose): `ValidMod S r` says that for every input `env` on which `r.lhs` is admissible under `S`, there is a form `v` of `r.lhs` permitted by `S` such that `r.rhs.eval env = v.eval env`, or the two are equal up to the sign of zero if `nsz ∈ S`. With no flags this is equality (`=` on `FP f`) on all inputs (`valid_iff_forall_eq`, proved). Validity is monotone in the flag set (`validMod_mono`, proved).

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Lemmas.lean:128-129
theorem valid_iff_forall_eq (r : Rewrite f n) :
    Valid r ↔ ∀ env, r.rhs.eval env = r.lhs.eval env := by …
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Lemmas.lean:159-160
theorem validMod_mono {S T : Flags} (hST : Flags.Sub S T) {r : Rewrite f n}
    (h : ValidMod S r) : ValidMod T r := by …
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Lemmas.lean:168-170
theorem validMod_iff_sub_of_minimal {S : Flags} {r : Rewrite f n}
    (hS : ValidMod S r) (hmin : ∀ fl, S.has fl = true → ¬ ValidMod (Flags.allBut fl) r)
    (T : Flags) : ValidMod T r ↔ Flags.Sub S T := by …
```

Concrete formats (all exhaustively enumerable):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Instances.lean:17
def F33 : Fmt := ⟨3, 3, by decide, by decide⟩
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Instances.lean:21
def F22 : Fmt := ⟨2, 2, by decide, by decide⟩
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Instances.lean:27
def F32 : Fmt := ⟨3, 2, by decide, by decide⟩
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Instances.lean:45
def dy (f : Fmt) (m : Int) (k : Int) : FP f := ofDyadic f (decide (m < 0)) m.natAbs k
```

Vocabulary used by the statements:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:81
def assocRoundA (f : Fmt) : FP f := ofDyadic f false 1 ((1 : Int) - f.bias + f.p)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:83
def assocRoundB (f : Fmt) : FP f := ofDyadic f false 1 ((1 : Int) - f.bias)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:87-88
def PermittedFormsAgree {n : Nat} (S : Flags) (e : Expr f n) : Prop :=
  ∀ env, ∀ v ∈ e.variants S, v.eval env = e.eval env
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:92-93
def OneNaNOrOppositeZeros (a b : FP f) : Prop :=
  a.isNaN ≠ b.isNaN ∨ (a.isZero = true ∧ b.isZero = true ∧ a ≠ b)
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:96-97
def PermittedFormsAgreeAt {n : Nat} (S : Flags) (e : Expr f n) (env : Fin n → FP f) : Prop :=
  ∀ v ∈ e.variants S, v.eval env = e.eval env
```

### Results

#### 1. `add_mul_fma_comm`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model (one NaN, payload and sign not represented), for all values x, y, z including NaN, ±∞ and ±0: add x y = add y x, mul x y = mul y x, and fma x y z = fma y x z (the two multiplicands of fma swapped), as identity of encodings.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_comm_mul_comm_fma_comm`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:25-26
theorem add_comm_mul_comm_fma_comm (x y z : FP f) :
    add x y = add y x ∧ mul x y = mul y x ∧ fma x y z = fma y x z := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3), distinct finite operands with rounding: 2 + 1/4 (a tie, = 2) in both orders; 1.25 * 1.5 (= 1.875, rounds to 2) in both orders; fma(5/16, 7/8, 2) with the multiplicands swapped. (`nv_comm_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:21-26
theorem nv_comm_F33 :
    dy F33 2 0 ≠ dy F33 1 (-2) ∧ dy F33 5 (-2) ≠ dy F33 3 (-1) ∧
    add (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2)) = dy F33 2 0 ∧ mul (dy F33 5 (-2)) (dy F33 3 (-1)) = dy F33 2 0 ∧
    add (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2)) = add (dy F33 1 (-2)) (dy F33 2 0) ∧
    mul (dy F33 5 (-2)) (dy F33 3 (-1)) = mul (dy F33 3 (-1)) (dy F33 5 (-2)) ∧
    fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0) = fma (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 2 0) := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_mul_fma_comm`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019, IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic, IEEE, July 2019, DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2019.8766229 (the 2019 text was not accessible). §6.3 'The sign bit', quoted from public verbatim quotations of the IEEE 754-2008 §6.3 text: an exactly zero sum of operands with opposite signs is +0 in every rounding-direction attribute except roundTowardNegative, and 'x + x = x − (−x) retains the same sign as x even when x is zero'; the sign of a product is the exclusive OR of the operand signs, also when the result is zero; for fusedMultiplyAdd an exactly zero (a×b)+c is signed by the sum rule, and a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero takes the sign of the exact result. §6.3 concerns non-comparison operations.
- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (quoted from the edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › Ambiguity': 'Due to roundoff errors, the associative laws of algebra do not necessarily hold for floating-point numbers', with (x+y)+z = 1 but x+(y+z) = 0 for x = 10^30, y = −10^30, z = 1.
- Sylvie Boldo, Jacques-Henri Jourdan, Xavier Leroy, Guillaume Melquiond, 'Verified Compilation of Floating-Point Computations', Journal of Automated Reasoning 54(2):135–163, 2015, DOI 10.1007/s10817-014-9317-x. Mechanized IEEE 754 semantics in CompCert (Coq/Flocq) with strict, value-preserving floating-point optimizations.
- Mechanized IEEE 754 models (none of which this package depends on): John Harrison, 'A Machine-Checked Theory of Floating Point Arithmetic', TPHOLs 1999, LNCS 1690, pp. 113–130, DOI 10.1007/3-540-48256-3_9; Lei Yu, 'A Formal Model of IEEE Floating Point Arithmetic', Archive of Formal Proofs, July 2013 (entry IEEE_Floating_Point); Lean 4 v4.34.1 core Init.Data.Float.Model (a parametric model with structure Format { mantissaBitsWithoutImplicit, exponentBits, he : 2 ≤ exponentBits } backing Float and Float32; inspected in the pinned toolchain source); FloatLib (R. J. George, W. Adkisson, A. Anandkumar, 'FloatLib: Verified Floating-Point Arithmetic in Lean', arXiv:2609.19352, 2026); FloatSpec (github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/FloatSpec, a Lean 4 port of the Coq Flocq library). No bridge between Toy.Float.FP and any of these is proved.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 2. `add_negZero_mul_one_identity`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for every value x (including NaN, ±∞ and both zeros): x + (−0) = x and x * 1 = x, as identity of encodings.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_negZero_right_and_mul_one_right`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:63-64
theorem add_negZero_right_and_mul_one_right (x : FP f) :
    add x (negZero f) = x ∧ mul x (one f) = x := …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): (−0) + (−0) = −0, (1/16) + (−0) = 1/16, (−∞) * 1 = −∞, NaN * 1 = NaN. (`nv_identities_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:31-33
theorem nv_identities_F33 :
    add (negZero F33) (negZero F33) = negZero F33 ∧ add (dy F33 1 (-4)) (negZero F33) = dy F33 1 (-4) ∧
    mul (.inf true) (one F33) = .inf true ∧ mul .nan (one F33) = .nan := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_negZero_mul_one_identity`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019, IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic, IEEE, July 2019, DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2019.8766229 (the 2019 text was not accessible). §6.3 'The sign bit', quoted from public verbatim quotations of the IEEE 754-2008 §6.3 text: an exactly zero sum of operands with opposite signs is +0 in every rounding-direction attribute except roundTowardNegative, and 'x + x = x − (−x) retains the same sign as x even when x is zero'; the sign of a product is the exclusive OR of the operand signs, also when the result is zero; for fusedMultiplyAdd an exactly zero (a×b)+c is signed by the sum rule, and a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero takes the sign of the exact result. §6.3 concerns non-comparison operations.
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.
- Sylvie Boldo, Jacques-Henri Jourdan, Xavier Leroy, Guillaume Melquiond, 'Verified Compilation of Floating-Point Computations', Journal of Automated Reasoning 54(2):135–163, 2015, DOI 10.1007/s10817-014-9317-x. Mechanized IEEE 754 semantics in CompCert (Coq/Flocq) with strict, value-preserving floating-point optimizations.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 3. `add_posZero_identity_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x + (+0) = x holds if and only if x ≠ −0 (at x = −0 the sum is +0), and x + (+0) equals x up to the sign of zero (EqNsz) for every x.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_posZero_right_eq_iff_and_eqNsz`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:69-70
theorem add_posZero_right_eq_iff_and_eqNsz (x : FP f) :
    (add x (posZero f) = x ↔ x ≠ negZero f) ∧ EqNsz (add x (posZero f)) x := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): (−0) + (+0) = +0 ≠ −0 (the failing instance) and (+0) + (+0) = +0 (a holding instance). (`add_posZero_fails_at_negZero_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:38-40
theorem add_posZero_fails_at_negZero_F33 :
    add (negZero F33) (posZero F33) = posZero F33 ∧ add (negZero F33) (posZero F33) ≠ negZero F33 ∧
    add (posZero F33) (posZero F33) = posZero F33 := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_posZero_identity_characterization`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.2 'Expression transformations' (HTML rendering of N1570). 'x - x → 0.0 The expressions x - x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN or infinite'. '0 * x → 0.0 The expressions 0*x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN, infinite, or -0' (this lists sufficient reasons; every negative finite x also gives −0). 'x + 0 → x The expressions x + 0 and x are not equivalent if x is -0, because (-0) + (+0) yields +0 (in the default rounding direction), not -0'. '-x ↔ 0 - x The expressions -x and 0 - x are not equivalent if x is +0, because -(+0) yields -0, but 0 - (+0) yields +0 (unless rounding is downward)'.
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- IEEE Std 754-2019, IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic, IEEE, July 2019, DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2019.8766229 (the 2019 text was not accessible). §6.3 'The sign bit', quoted from public verbatim quotations of the IEEE 754-2008 §6.3 text: an exactly zero sum of operands with opposite signs is +0 in every rounding-direction attribute except roundTowardNegative, and 'x + x = x − (−x) retains the same sign as x even when x is zero'; the sign of a product is the exclusive OR of the operand signs, also when the result is zero; for fusedMultiplyAdd an exactly zero (a×b)+c is signed by the sum rule, and a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero takes the sign of the exact result. §6.3 concerns non-comparison operations.
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 4. `sub_self_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x − x = +0 if and only if x is finite (this includes x = ±0), and x − x is NaN if and only if x is not finite (x is NaN, +∞ or −∞).

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.sub_self_eq_posZero_iff_and_eq_nan_iff`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:87-88
theorem sub_self_eq_posZero_iff_and_eq_nan_iff (x : FP f) :
    (sub x x = posZero f ↔ x.isFinite = true) ∧ (sub x x = .nan ↔ x.isFinite = false) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): NaN − NaN = NaN, ∞ − ∞ = NaN (the input +∞ is not NaN; the result is), (−∞) − (−∞) = NaN, (−0) − (−0) = +0. (`sub_self_fails_at_nan_inf_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:46-49
theorem sub_self_fails_at_nan_inf_F33 :
    sub (.nan : FP F33) .nan = .nan ∧ sub (.inf false : FP F33) (.inf false) = .nan ∧
    sub (.inf true : FP F33) (.inf true) = .nan ∧ (FP.inf false : FP F33).isNaN = false ∧
    sub (negZero F33) (negZero F33) = posZero F33 := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `sub_self_characterization`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.2 'Expression transformations' (HTML rendering of N1570). 'x - x → 0.0 The expressions x - x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN or infinite'. '0 * x → 0.0 The expressions 0*x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN, infinite, or -0' (this lists sufficient reasons; every negative finite x also gives −0). 'x + 0 → x The expressions x + 0 and x are not equivalent if x is -0, because (-0) + (+0) yields +0 (in the default rounding direction), not -0'. '-x ↔ 0 - x The expressions -x and 0 - x are not equivalent if x is +0, because -(+0) yields -0, but 0 - (+0) yields +0 (unless rounding is downward)'.
- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › The IEEE Standard': 'Since x - x = +0 for all x, (+0) - (+0) = +0. However, -(+0) = -0, thus -x should not be defined as 0 - x', with footnote 27 'Unless the rounding mode is round toward -∞, in which case x - x = -0' (the sentence ignores NaN and infinities); the same paragraph: 'a NaN is never equal to any other number (including another NaN), so x = x is no longer always true' and 'NaNs are unordered with respect to all other numbers, so x ≤ y cannot be defined as not x > y'. Section 'The IEEE Standard › Special Quantities › Signed Zero': 'the IEEE standard defines comparison so that +0 = -0, rather than -0 < +0'.
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 5. `feq_self_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for every value x: the IEEE quiet equality x == x (feq) is true if and only if x is not NaN.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

```lean

theorem feq_self_iff (x : FP f) : feq x x = true ↔ x ≠ .nan := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019 §5.11 'Details of comparison predicates' (the standard's text was not accessible; quoted from a public verbatim quotation of the 754-2008 text and a late 754-2019 draft, Stack Overflow question 71224184, answer by E. Postpischil): 'Four mutually exclusive relations are possible: less than, equal, greater than, and unordered. The last case arises when at least one operand is NaN. Every NaN shall compare unordered with everything, including itself. Comparisons shall ignore the sign of zero (so +0 = −0).'
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.3 'Relational operators' (HTML rendering of N1570; the same text is F.8.3 in C99 N1256). 'x != x → false The expression x != x is true if x is a NaN.' 'x == x → true The expression x = x [sic; C99 N1256 F.8.3 reads 'The statement x == x'] is false if x is a NaN.' 'The sense of relational operators shall be maintained. This includes handling unordered cases as expressed by the source code.' EXAMPLE: 'if (a < b) f(); else g();' is not equivalent to 'if (a >= b) g(); else f();' (and the comments note that both raise 'invalid' if a and b are unordered) but is equivalent to 'if (!(a < b)) g(); else f();'.
- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › The IEEE Standard': 'Since x - x = +0 for all x, (+0) - (+0) = +0. However, -(+0) = -0, thus -x should not be defined as 0 - x', with footnote 27 'Unless the rounding mode is round toward -∞, in which case x - x = -0' (the sentence ignores NaN and infinities); the same paragraph: 'a NaN is never equal to any other number (including another NaN), so x = x is no longer always true' and 'NaNs are unordered with respect to all other numbers, so x ≤ y cannot be defined as not x > y'. Section 'The IEEE Standard › Special Quantities › Signed Zero': 'the IEEE standard defines comparison so that +0 = -0, rather than -0 < +0'.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 6. `mul_posZero_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for every value x: x * (+0) = +0 if and only if x is finite and its sign bit is clear; if x is not finite (NaN, +∞ or −∞), x * (+0) is NaN; and if x is finite with the sign bit set (−0 and every negative finite value), x * (+0) is −0.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.mul_posZero_eq_posZero_iff_and_failure_values`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:115-118
theorem mul_posZero_eq_posZero_iff_and_failure_values (x : FP f) :
    (mul x (posZero f) = posZero f ↔ x.isFinite = true ∧ x.signBit = false) ∧
    (x.isFinite = false → mul x (posZero f) = .nan) ∧
    (x.isFinite = true → x.signBit = true → mul x (posZero f) = negZero f) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): NaN, +∞, −∞ give NaN; −1 and −0 give −0; 3 * (+0) = +0. (`mul_posZero_fails_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:58-62
theorem mul_posZero_fails_F33 :
    mul (.nan : FP F33) (posZero F33) = .nan ∧ mul (.inf false : FP F33) (posZero F33) = .nan ∧
    mul (.inf true : FP F33) (posZero F33) = .nan ∧
    mul (one F33).neg (posZero F33) = negZero F33 ∧ mul (negZero F33) (posZero F33) = negZero F33 ∧
    mul (dy F33 3 0) (posZero F33) = posZero F33 := by …
```

#### 7. `add_assoc_fails_by_rounding`

**Plain claim.** For every format with p + 1 ≤ 2·bias, the exposed witnesses a = 2^(emin+p) and b = 2^emin (emin = 1 − bias; assocRoundA, assocRoundB) satisfy all of the following: a, b, a + b, b + b, (a + b) + b and a + (b + b) are normal; (a + b) + b = a; and a + (b + b) ≠ a. So rounding alone breaks associativity of add, with no special values, underflow or overflow involved.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_of_prec_le_two_bias`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:132-139
theorem add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_of_prec_le_two_bias (f : Fmt)
    (h : f.p + 1 ≤ 2 * f.bias) :
    [assocRoundA f, assocRoundB f, add (assocRoundA f) (assocRoundB f),
      add (assocRoundB f) (assocRoundB f),
      add (add (assocRoundA f) (assocRoundB f)) (assocRoundB f),
      add (assocRoundA f) (add (assocRoundB f) (assocRoundB f))].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
    add (add (assocRoundA f) (assocRoundB f)) (assocRoundB f) = assocRoundA f ∧
    add (assocRoundA f) (add (assocRoundB f) (assocRoundB f)) ≠ assocRoundA f := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `h : f.p + 1 ≤ 2 * f.bias` — Makes a + 2b = 2^(emin+p)(1 + 2^(1−p)) a finite normal value (emin + p ≤ emax). **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_hyp_tight_F22` (witness class: the format (2,2) with the same exposed witnesses assocRoundA, assocRoundB (there assocRoundA overflows to +∞); tightness is recorded for these witnesses; for other witnesses it is neither established nor refuted here); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `h : f.p + 1 ≤ 2 * f.bias`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:70-77
theorem add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_hyp_tight_F22 :
    ¬ (F22.p + 1 ≤ 2 * F22.bias) ∧
    ¬ ([assocRoundA F22, assocRoundB F22, add (assocRoundA F22) (assocRoundB F22),
        add (assocRoundB F22) (assocRoundB F22),
        add (add (assocRoundA F22) (assocRoundB F22)) (assocRoundB F22),
        add (assocRoundA F22) (add (assocRoundB F22) (assocRoundB F22))].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
      add (add (assocRoundA F22) (assocRoundB F22)) (assocRoundB F22) = assocRoundA F22 ∧
      add (assocRoundA F22) (add (assocRoundB F22) (assocRoundB F22)) ≠ assocRoundA F22) := by …
```

**Non-vacuity witness.** Formats (3,2) and (3,3) satisfy h. In (3,3): a = 2, b = 1/4; (2 + 1/4) + 1/4 = 2 and 2 + (1/4 + 1/4) = 2.5. (`add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_F32_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:333-347
theorem add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_F32_F33 :
    (F32.p + 1 ≤ 2 * F32.bias ∧
      [assocRoundA F32, assocRoundB F32, add (assocRoundA F32) (assocRoundB F32),
        add (assocRoundB F32) (assocRoundB F32),
        add (add (assocRoundA F32) (assocRoundB F32)) (assocRoundB F32),
        add (assocRoundA F32) (add (assocRoundB F32) (assocRoundB F32))].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
      add (add (assocRoundA F32) (assocRoundB F32)) (assocRoundB F32) = assocRoundA F32 ∧
      add (assocRoundA F32) (add (assocRoundB F32) (assocRoundB F32)) ≠ assocRoundA F32) ∧
    (F33.p + 1 ≤ 2 * F33.bias ∧
      [assocRoundA F33, assocRoundB F33, add (assocRoundA F33) (assocRoundB F33),
        add (assocRoundB F33) (assocRoundB F33),
        add (add (assocRoundA F33) (assocRoundB F33)) (assocRoundB F33),
        add (assocRoundA F33) (add (assocRoundB F33) (assocRoundB F33))].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
      add (add (assocRoundA F33) (assocRoundB F33)) (assocRoundB F33) = assocRoundA F33 ∧
      add (assocRoundA F33) (add (assocRoundB F33) (assocRoundB F33)) ≠ assocRoundA F33) := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_assoc_fails_by_rounding`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (quoted from the edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › Ambiguity': 'Due to roundoff errors, the associative laws of algebra do not necessarily hold for floating-point numbers', with (x+y)+z = 1 but x+(y+z) = 0 for x = 10^30, y = −10^30, z = 1.

**Design question.** Which algebraic rewrites may an optimizer apply to IEEE 754 binary arithmetic with round-to-nearest-even without any fast-math flag, and which special values (signed zero, NaN, infinities, subnormals) are responsible for each failure?

#### 8. `add_assoc_fails_by_overflow`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, with M the largest finite value: (M + M) + (−M) = +∞ while M + (M + (−M)) = M.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:223-225
theorem add_assoc_fails_by_overflow (f : Fmt) :
    add (add (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f false)) (maxFinite f true) = .inf false ∧
    add (maxFinite f false) (add (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f true)) = maxFinite f false := by …
```

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** No hypotheses beyond the format invariants; in format (3,3), M = 14 (add_assoc_overflow_excluded_by_ninf_F33 evaluates the instance). (`add_assoc_overflow_excluded_by_ninf_F33`)

Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › Ambiguity': 'Due to roundoff errors, the associative laws of algebra do not necessarily hold for floating-point numbers', with (x+y)+z = 1 but x+(y+z) = 0 for x = 10^30, y = −10^30, z = 1.

#### 9. `add_assoc_zero_sign_impossible`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, gradual underflow), for all a, b, c: if (a + b) + c and a + (b + c) are equal up to the sign of zero, then they are identical. So under round-to-nearest-even with gradual underflow the two bracketings of a sum of three values never differ only in the sign of zero.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** impossibility / T1 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_assoc_defect_never_only_zero_sign`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:235-236
theorem add_assoc_defect_never_only_zero_sign (a b c : FP f)
    (h : EqNsz (add (add a b) c) (add a (add b c))) : add (add a b) c = add a (add b c) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `h : EqNsz (add (add a b) c) (add a (add b c))` — Restricts to triples where the bracketings agree up to zero sign; without it the conclusion is associativity, which fails. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `add_assoc_fails_F33` (witness class: an all-normal triple (2, 1/4, 1/4) in format (3,3), where the bracketings give 2 and 2.5); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `h : EqNsz (add (add a b) c) (add a (add b c))`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:148-154
theorem add_assoc_fails_F33 :
    add (add (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) (dy F33 1 (-2)) ≠
      add (dy F33 2 0) (add (dy F33 1 (-2)) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
    ¬ EqNsz (add (add (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) (dy F33 1 (-2)))
      (add (dy F33 2 0) (add (dy F33 1 (-2)) (dy F33 1 (-2)))) ∧
    add (add (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) (dy F33 1 (-2)) = dy F33 2 0 ∧
    add (dy F33 2 0) (add (dy F33 1 (-2)) (dy F33 1 (-2))) = dy F33 5 (-1) := by …
```

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3), (a, b, c) = (−0, +0, −0): h holds and both bracketings are +0. (`nv_assoc_zero_sign_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:133-135
theorem nv_assoc_zero_sign_F33 :
    EqNsz (add (add (negZero F33) (posZero F33)) (negZero F33))
      (add (negZero F33) (add (posZero F33) (negZero F33))) := by …
```

**Positive control.** (`sub_rebracket_differs_only_in_zero_sign_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:141-143
theorem sub_rebracket_differs_only_in_zero_sign_F33 :
    sub (sub (negZero F33) (posZero F33)) (negZero F33) = posZero F33 ∧
    sub (negZero F33) (add (posZero F33) (negZero F33)) = negZero F33 := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_assoc_zero_sign_impossible`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019, IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic, IEEE, July 2019, DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2019.8766229 (the 2019 text was not accessible). §6.3 'The sign bit', quoted from public verbatim quotations of the IEEE 754-2008 §6.3 text: an exactly zero sum of operands with opposite signs is +0 in every rounding-direction attribute except roundTowardNegative, and 'x + x = x − (−x) retains the same sign as x even when x is zero'; the sign of a product is the exclusive OR of the operand signs, also when the result is zero; for fusedMultiplyAdd an exactly zero (a×b)+c is signed by the sum rule, and a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero takes the sign of the exact result. §6.3 concerns non-comparison operations.
- Rust core library, library/core/src/primitive_docs.rs at rust-lang/rust commit 268bdaabaceb7688b974bae0f96b9c5c1b6ab991 (lines 1194–1207): primitive operations round with roundTiesToEven, and 'If the result of a sum exactly equals zero, the outcome is +0.0 unless both arguments were negative, then it is -0.0. Subtraction a - b is regarded as a sum a + (-b).'

**Design question.** May an optimizer that ignores the sign of zero (LLVM nsz) but not other differences re-bracket floating-point sums, i.e. can re-bracketing ever change only the sign of a zero result?

#### 10. `add_assoc_survives_restriction_flags`

**Plain claim.** In format (3,3), at the exposed input (x, y, z) = (2, 1/4, 1/4) (envAssocRound): the source (x + y) + z is admissible under the flag set {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract} (no NaN or infinite argument or result), evaluates to 2, the target x + (y + z) evaluates to 2.5, and the target is not related by Rel {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract} to the value of any form of the source generated by Expr.variants {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract}; hence (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) is not ValidMod {nnan, ninf, nsz, contract}. So in this model reassoc is not implied by the other flags. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.add_assoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:106-113
theorem add_assoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33 :
    Admissible (Flags.allBut .reassoc) (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs envAssocRound ∧
    (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs.eval envAssocRound = dy F33 2 0 ∧
    (rwAddAssoc F33).rhs.eval envAssocRound = dy F33 5 (-1) ∧
    (∀ v ∈ (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs.variants (Flags.allBut .reassoc),
      ¬ Rel (Flags.allBut .reassoc) ((rwAddAssoc F33).rhs.eval envAssocRound)
        (v.eval envAssocRound)) ∧
    ¬ ValidMod (Flags.allBut .reassoc) (rwAddAssoc F33) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** The exposed input (2, 1/4, 1/4) in format (3,3) is admissible under all flags except reassoc (first conjunct). (`add_assoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33`)

    (rwAddAssoc F33).rhs.eval envAssocRound = dy F33 5 (-1) ∧
    (∀ v ∈ (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs.variants (Flags.allBut .reassoc),
      ¬ Rel (Flags.allBut .reassoc) ((rwAddAssoc F33).rhs.eval envAssocRound)
        (v.eval envAssocRound)) ∧
    ¬ ValidMod (Flags.allBut .reassoc) (rwAddAssoc F33) := by …

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `add_assoc_survives_restriction_flags`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (quoted from the edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › Ambiguity': 'Due to roundoff errors, the associative laws of algebra do not necessarily hold for floating-point numbers', with (x+y)+z = 1 but x+(y+z) = 0 for x = 10^30, y = −10^30, z = 1.
- LLVM Language Reference Manual, llvm/docs/LangRef.md at llvm/llvm-project commit a2f1d1aeb6f5c700ac9a15592b6f81c3ec41e6c6, section 'Fast-Math Flags' (lines 4466–4566): reassoc 'Allow algebraically equivalent transformations for floating-point instructions such as reassociation transformations. This may dramatically change results in floating-point.'
- Heiko Becker, Eva Darulova, Magnus O. Myreen, Zachary Tatlock, 'Icing: Supporting Fast-Math Style Optimizations in a Verified Compiler', Computer Aided Verification (CAV 2019), LNCS 11562, pp. 155–173, Springer, 2019, DOI 10.1007/978-3-030-25543-5_10. Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).

**Design question.** Which fast-math flags license reassociation of floating-point sums, and can the restriction flags nnan, ninf, nsz substitute for reassoc?

#### 11. `permissions_refine_iff_laws`

**Plain claim.** (Pointwise characterization.) In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: every form of (x + y) + z generated by Expr.variants {reassoc} evaluates at (a, b, c) to the value of (x + y) + z if and only if (a + b) + c = a + (b + c); and every form of x * y + z generated by Expr.variants {contract} evaluates at (a, b, c) to the value of x * y + z if and only if fma a b c = a * b + c. (Witnesses in every format.) With M the largest finite value: (M + M) + (−M) = +∞ while its permitted form M + (M + (−M)) = M, and M * 2 + (−M) = +∞ while its permitted form fma M 2 (−M) = M; so at these exposed inputs not every permitted form agrees with the source, in every format.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statements** (`Toy.Float.permittedFormsAgreeAt_iff_laws`, `Toy.Float.reassoc_contract_change_result_at_maxFinite`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:258-262
theorem permittedFormsAgreeAt_iff_laws (a b c : FP f) :
    (PermittedFormsAgreeAt fReassoc (rwAddAssoc f).lhs (env3 a b c) ↔
      add (add a b) c = add a (add b c)) ∧
    (PermittedFormsAgreeAt fContract (rwContract f).lhs (env3 a b c) ↔
      fma a b c = add (mul a b) c) := by …
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:328-336
theorem reassoc_contract_change_result_at_maxFinite (f : Fmt) :
    add (add (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f false)) (maxFinite f true) = .inf false ∧
    add (maxFinite f false) (add (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f true)) = maxFinite f false ∧
    add (mul (maxFinite f false) (ofDyadic f false 1 1)) (maxFinite f true) = .inf false ∧
    fma (maxFinite f false) (ofDyadic f false 1 1) (maxFinite f true) = maxFinite f false ∧
    ¬ PermittedFormsAgreeAt fReassoc (rwAddAssoc f).lhs
        (env3 (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f false) (maxFinite f true)) ∧
    ¬ PermittedFormsAgreeAt fContract (rwContract f).lhs
        (env3 (maxFinite f false) (ofDyadic f false 1 1) (maxFinite f true)) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): both pointwise agreements hold at (+0, +0, +0) and both fail at (2, 1/4, 1/4) for reassoc and at (5/16, 7/8, 2) for contract, so both sides of both equivalences take both truth values. (`nv_permittedFormsAgreeAt_true_and_false_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:362-369
theorem nv_permittedFormsAgreeAt_true_and_false_F33 :
    PermittedFormsAgreeAt fReassoc (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs
      (env3 (posZero F33) (posZero F33) (posZero F33)) ∧
    PermittedFormsAgreeAt fContract (rwContract F33).lhs
      (env3 (posZero F33) (posZero F33) (posZero F33)) ∧
    ¬ PermittedFormsAgreeAt fReassoc (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs envAssocRound ∧
    ¬ PermittedFormsAgreeAt fContract (rwContract F33).lhs
        (env3 (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)) := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `permissions_refine_iff_laws`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- LLVM Language Reference Manual, llvm/docs/LangRef.md at llvm/llvm-project commit a2f1d1aeb6f5c700ac9a15592b6f81c3ec41e6c6, section 'Fast-Math Flags': contract 'Allow floating-point contraction (e.g., fusing a multiply followed by an addition into a fused multiply-and-add)'; reassoc 'Allow algebraically equivalent transformations ... This may dramatically change results in floating-point.'
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), HTML rendering. §6.5 ¶8: 'A floating expression may be contracted, that is, evaluated as though it were a single operation, thereby omitting rounding errors implied by the source code'. §7.12.2 ¶2: the FP_CONTRACT pragma allows or disallows contraction, with implementation-defined default.
- Heiko Becker, Eva Darulova, Magnus O. Myreen, Zachary Tatlock, 'Icing: Supporting Fast-Math Style Optimizations in a Verified Compiler', Computer Aided Verification (CAV 2019), LNCS 11562, pp. 155–173, Springer, 2019, DOI 10.1007/978-3-030-25543-5_10. Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.

**Design question.** Are the permissions reassoc and contract refinements of the flag-free semantics (do they ever change results), and on exactly which inputs do their permitted forms agree with the source?

#### 12. `fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal`

**Plain claim.** In format (3,3), for a = 5/16, b = 7/8, c = 2: the exact product a·b is 70 units of 2^−8 (35/128, at least the smallest normal 1/4, so no underflow), the rounded product a * b is 1/4, the inputs, the rounded product and both results are normal, fma a b c = 2.5, and a * b + c = 2. So contraction changes results on finite inputs by rounding alone.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:178-186
theorem fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33 :
    F33.fracCount * 2 ^ F33.qneg ≤ (dy F33 5 (-4)).units * (dy F33 7 (-3)).units ∧
    (dy F33 5 (-4)).units * (dy F33 7 (-3)).units = 70 ∧
    mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) = dy F33 1 (-2) ∧
    [dy F33 5 (-4), dy F33 7 (-3), dy F33 2 0, mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)),
      fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0),
      add (mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3))) (dy F33 2 0)].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
    fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0) = dy F33 5 (-1) ∧
    add (mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3))) (dy F33 2 0) = dy F33 2 0 := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** The instance itself. (`fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33`)
