```lean
    F33.fracCount * 2 ^ F33.qneg ≤ (dy F33 5 (-4)).units * (dy F33 7 (-3)).units ∧
    (dy F33 5 (-4)).units * (dy F33 7 (-3)).units = 70 ∧
    mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) = dy F33 1 (-2) ∧
    [dy F33 5 (-4), dy F33 7 (-3), dy F33 2 0, mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)),
      fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0),
      add (mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3))) (dy F33 2 0)].all FP.isNormal = true ∧
    fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0) = dy F33 5 (-1) ∧
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2008 §5.4.1, quoted verbatim in a public source: fusedMultiplyAdd(x, y, z) 'computes (x×y)+z as if with unbounded range and precision, rounding only once to the destination format ... and so fusedMultiplyAdd differs from a multiplication operation followed by an addition operation'. The 2019 wording and section number were not checked at a 2019 source.
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), HTML rendering. §6.5 ¶8 (contraction) and Annex F.7 ¶1: a contracted expression is correctly rounded once.

**Design question.** When a compiler contracts a*b + c into a fused multiply-add (C FP_CONTRACT, LLVM contract), what is gained (accuracy) and what is lost (refinement of the unfused semantics)?

#### 13. `fma_nearest`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c and every value r: if fma a b c is finite, then its absolute error with respect to the exact a·b + c is at most the error of r (the error of r is infinite when r is NaN or ±∞). So a finite fused result is a nearest value of the format to the exact a·b + c.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / substantive / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.fma_nearest_of_fma_finite`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:430-431
theorem fma_nearest_of_fma_finite (a b c r : FP f) (h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true) :
    ErrLe (errOf (fma a b c) (exactMulAdd a b c)) (errOf r (exactMulAdd a b c)) := by …
```

**Proof outline.** If `fma a b c` is finite, then `a`, `b` and `c` are finite (`fma_isFinite_imp`). If the exact value `exactMulAdd a b c` is `0`, the result is a signed zero with error `0`. Otherwise `fma a b c` is `round f (decide (v < 0)) v.natAbs f.qneg` for the exact value `v` (`fma_eq_round_exactMulAdd`). Its magnitude `roundUnits f v.natAbs f.qneg` is at least as close to `v.natAbs / 2^qneg` as every magnitude with at most `p` significant bits, at any exponent (`roundUnits_nearest`; `IsRepr f n` says that `n` is such a magnitude, and the magnitude of every finite value is one, `unitsOf_isRepr`). A finite value of the opposite sign is no closer than `0`, which is itself such a magnitude. `fma_err_le` states the resulting inequality for every finite `r`; a non-finite `r` has infinite error.

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true` — A fused result that overflows to ±∞ has infinite error and is not nearest to a finite exact value. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33` (witness class: the format (3,3) instance (a, b, c) = (3/4, 12, 6) with r = 14 (the two-step result): fma overflows to +∞ and 14 has finite error); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:197-204
theorem fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33 :
    fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = .inf false ∧
    add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0) = dy F33 14 0 ∧
    exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = 15 * 2 ^ 8 ∧
    ¬ ErrLe (errOf (fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0)))
        (errOf (add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))) := by …
```

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3), (5/16, 7/8, 2): fused result 2.5 (finite), error 58 units of 2^−8 (29/128). (`nv_fma_accuracy_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:211-216
theorem nv_fma_accuracy_F33 :
    (fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)).isFinite = true ∧
    errOf (fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0))
      (exactMulAdd (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)) = some 58 ∧
    errOf (add (mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3))) (dy F33 2 0))
      (exactMulAdd (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)) = some 70 := by …
```

**Bounded cross-checks** (kernel evaluation by `decide +kernel` over exactly the operands stated; the general theorem above is proved for every format, and these instances are independent exhaustive checks in small formats):

```lean
    ∀ a b c : FP F22, (fma a b c).isFinite = true → ∀ r : FP F22,
      ErrLe (errOf (fma a b c) (exactMulAdd a b c)) (errOf r (exactMulAdd a b c)) := by …
```

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2008 §5.4.1, quoted verbatim in a public source: fusedMultiplyAdd(x, y, z) 'computes (x×y)+z as if with unbounded range and precision, rounding only once to the destination format ... and so fusedMultiplyAdd differs from a multiplication operation followed by an addition operation'. The 2019 wording and section number were not checked at a 2019 source.
- Sylvie Boldo and Guillaume Melquiond, 'Flocq: A Unified Library for Proving Floating-Point Algorithms in Coq', 2011 IEEE 20th Symposium on Computer Arithmetic (ARITH), pp. 243–252, DOI 10.1109/ARITH.2011.40 (a general mechanized formalization of floating-point rounding; bibliographic data verified, specific theorems not checked).
- Mechanized IEEE 754 models (none of which this package depends on): John Harrison, 'A Machine-Checked Theory of Floating Point Arithmetic', TPHOLs 1999, LNCS 1690, pp. 113–130, DOI 10.1007/3-540-48256-3_9; Lei Yu, 'A Formal Model of IEEE Floating Point Arithmetic', Archive of Formal Proofs, July 2013 (entry IEEE_Floating_Point); Lean 4 v4.34.1 core Init.Data.Float.Model (a parametric model with structure Format { mantissaBitsWithoutImplicit, exponentBits, he : 2 ≤ exponentBits } backing Float and Float32; inspected in the pinned toolchain source); FloatLib (R. J. George, W. Adkisson, A. Anandkumar, 'FloatLib: Verified Floating-Point Arithmetic in Lean', arXiv:2609.19352, 2026); FloatSpec (github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/FloatSpec, a Lean 4 port of the Coq Flocq library). No bridge between Toy.Float.FP and any of these is proved.

**Design question.** When a compiler contracts a*b + c into a fused multiply-add (C FP_CONTRACT, LLVM contract), what is gained (accuracy) and what is lost (refinement of the unfused semantics)?

#### 14. `fma_errLe_twoStep`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: if fma a b c is finite, its error with respect to the exact a·b + c is at most the error of the two-step a * b + c (infinite if the two-step result overflows). Finiteness of the two-step result is not a sufficient hypothesis: in format (3,3), for (3/4, 12, 6) the two-step result is 14 while the fused result overflows to +∞, and the fused error is not at most the two-step error.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T1 / definitional / proved (one-line corollary of fma_nearest_of_fma_finite)

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statements** (`Toy.Float.fma_errLe_twoStep_of_fma_finite`, `Toy.Float.fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:441-442
theorem fma_errLe_twoStep_of_fma_finite (a b c : FP f) (h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true) :
    ErrLe (errOf (fma a b c) (exactMulAdd a b c)) (errOf (add (mul a b) c) (exactMulAdd a b c)) := …
```

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:197-204
theorem fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33 :
    fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = .inf false ∧
    add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0) = dy F33 14 0 ∧
    exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = 15 * 2 ^ 8 ∧
    ¬ ErrLe (errOf (fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0)))
        (errOf (add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true` — Excludes the overflow corner where rounding the exact value up crosses the overflow threshold while the two-step evaluation, having rounded the product down, stays finite. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33` (witness class: the format (3,3) instance (a, b, c) = (3/4, 12, 6)); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `h : (fma a b c).isFinite = true`:

```lean
    fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = .inf false ∧
    add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0) = dy F33 14 0 ∧
    exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0) = 15 * 2 ^ 8 ∧
    ¬ ErrLe (errOf (fma (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0)))
        (errOf (add (mul (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0)) (dy F33 6 0))
          (exactMulAdd (dy F33 3 (-2)) (dy F33 12 0) (dy F33 6 0))) := by …
```

```lean

    (fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)).isFinite = true ∧
    errOf (fma (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0))
      (exactMulAdd (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)) = some 58 ∧
    errOf (add (mul (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3))) (dy F33 2 0))
      (exactMulAdd (dy F33 5 (-4)) (dy F33 7 (-3)) (dy F33 2 0)) = some 70 := by …
```

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2008 §5.4.1, quoted verbatim in a public source: fusedMultiplyAdd(x, y, z) 'computes (x×y)+z as if with unbounded range and precision, rounding only once to the destination format ... and so fusedMultiplyAdd differs from a multiplication operation followed by an addition operation'. The 2019 wording and section number were not checked at a 2019 source.
- IEEE Std 754-2019 §4.3.1, quoted in public sources: under roundTiesToEven 'an infinitely precise result with magnitude at least b^emax(b − ½b^(1−p)) shall round to ∞ with no change in sign'.

**Design question.** When a compiler contracts a*b + c into a fused multiply-add (C FP_CONTRACT, LLVM contract), what is gained (accuracy) and what is lost (refinement of the unfused semantics)?

#### 15. `contraction_hazard_mulSubMul`

**Plain claim.** In format (3,3), at a = 3/16, b = 3: the product a * b = 9/16 rounds to 1/2, the flag-free value of a*b − a*b is +0, and Expr.variants {contract} generates the form fma a b (−(a*b)) of a*b − a*b, whose value is 1/16 (the rounding error of the product). So contraction is not semantics-preserving even for an expression whose real value is 0.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.contract_mulSubMul_nonzero_F33`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:223-228
theorem contract_mulSubMul_nonzero_F33 :
    mul (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 0) = dy F33 1 (-1) ∧
    (eMulSubMul F33).eval (env2 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 0)) = posZero F33 ∧
    Expr.fma (.var 0) (.var 1) (.neg (.mul (.var 0) (.var 1))) ∈ (eMulSubMul F33).variants fContract ∧
    (Expr.fma (.var 0) (.var 1) (.neg (.mul (.var 0) (.var 1)))).eval
      (env2 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 0)) = dy F33 1 (-4) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** The instance itself. (`contract_mulSubMul_nonzero_F33`)

```lean
    Expr.fma (.var 0) (.var 1) (.neg (.mul (.var 0) (.var 1))) ∈ (eMulSubMul F33).variants fContract ∧
    (Expr.fma (.var 0) (.var 1) (.neg (.mul (.var 0) (.var 1)))).eval
      (env2 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 0)) = dy F33 1 (-4) := by …
```

- Rust RFC 3514 'float_semantics' (rust-lang/rfcs pull request #3514, merged 2024-07-27), text/3514-float-semantics.md at rust-lang/rfcs commit f17e8623ee2e2854570dcdb936a9f4ab08c0fcd4: strict IEEE semantics 'precludes many transformations, such as turning a*b + c into FMA operations', with the x*x - x*x versus x.mul_add(x, -x*x) example.

Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.

#### 16. `double_rounding_not_innocuous`

**Plain claim.** With narrow format (3,3) (p = 3) and wide format (3,4) (p' = 4): 3/16 + 2.5 computed by addVia (convert both operands to the wide format, add there, convert back, each step correctly rounded) is 3, while direct addition in the narrow format gives 2.5.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** counterexample / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

```lean
    addVia W34 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 5 (-1)) ≠ add (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 5 (-1)) ∧
    addVia W34 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 5 (-1)) = dy F33 3 0 ∧
    add (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 5 (-1)) = dy F33 5 (-1) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Prior art.**

- Samuel A. Figueroa, 'When is double rounding innocuous?', ACM SIGNUM Newsletter 30(3):21–26, July 1995, DOI 10.1145/221332.221334 (bibliographic data verified; the paper text was not accessible). Its theorems are restated in S. A. Figueroa del Cid, 'A Rigorous Framework for Fully Supporting the IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic in High-Level Programming Languages', PhD thesis, New York University, January 2000, §6.2, Theorems 1–5 and Table 6.1: for binary p-digit operands with round-to-nearest, rounding first to q digits is innocuous for addition iff q ≥ 2p + 1, for subtraction iff q ≥ 2p + 1 (ties to even), for multiplication (p ≥ 4) and division if q ≥ 2p, and for square root iff q ≥ 2p + 2; the proofs assume an unbounded exponent range.

**Design question.** When is evaluating a narrow-format operation in a wider format and rounding back (x87 extended precision, FLT_EVAL_METHOD, emulation) identical to evaluating it directly?

#### 17. `double_rounding_add_innocuous`

**Plain claim.** For all formats f (narrow, precision p) and g (wide, precision p'), if 2p + 1 ≤ p' and bias f ≤ bias g, then for all values x, y of f: addVia g x y (convert x and y to g, add in g, convert the sum back to f, each step correctly rounded with roundTiesToEven) equals add x y.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / substantive / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.addVia_eq_add_of_two_mul_add_one_le_and_bias_le`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:450-451
theorem addVia_eq_add_of_two_mul_add_one_le_and_bias_le (f g : Fmt) (hp : 2 * f.p + 1 ≤ g.p)
    (hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias) (x y : FP f) : addVia g x y = add x y := by …
```

**Proof outline.** Under `f.p ≤ g.p` and `f.bias ≤ g.bias`, widening a finite value from `f` to `g` is exact (`cvt_up_fin`), so the sum computed in `g` is the exact sum rescaled by `2^(g.qneg − f.qneg)`; the NaN, infinity and signed-zero cases agree case by case. For a nonzero exact sum of magnitude `A` (in units of `f`), the result via `g` is `roundUnits f (roundUnits g A 0) 0` and the direct result is `roundUnits f A 0`, and these are equal (`roundUnits_double_sum`). The core is `rneDiv_double`: rounding `A` first to a multiple of `2^t'` and then to a multiple of `2^t` (`t' < t`) gives the same result as rounding once to a multiple of `2^t`, unless `A` lies within `2^(t'−1)` of an odd multiple of `2^(t−1)` (a midpoint of the coarse grid) without being equal to it. `sum_far` excludes that case for a sum or difference of two magnitudes with `p` significant bits when `2p + 1 ≤ p'`, with `L` the bit length of `A`, `t = L − p` and `t' = L − p'`. Either both operand exponents are at least `t'`, so that `A` and every midpoint are multiples of `2^t'`; or the operand with the smaller exponent is below `2^(t−2)` (this step uses `2p + 1 ≤ p'`) and the other operand is a multiple of `2^(t−1)`. In the second case a midpoint within `2^(t'−1)` of `A` would have to equal that other operand, which is then an odd multiple of `2^(t−1)` with at most `p` significant bits and so lies at least `2^(t−1)` below `A`, a contradiction. If the sum overflows in `g`, the direct sum overflows in `f` too (`overflow_transfer`).

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `hp : 2 * f.p + 1 ≤ g.p` — Figueroa's precision bound for addition under round-to-nearest-even. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `addVia_ne_add_F33_W36` (witness class: the format pair f = (3,3), g = (3,6) (p' = 2p, same exponent range): 7/16 + 5 gives 6 via g and 5 directly); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.
- `hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias` — The exponent range of g contains that of f (narrow values convert to g exactly, and narrow overflow precedes wide overflow). **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `addVia_ne_add_F33_W29` (witness class: the format pair f = (3,3), g = (2,9): converting the input 4 to g already overflows to +∞, so 4 + 0 via g is +∞); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `hp : 2 * f.p + 1 ≤ g.p`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:256-260
theorem addVia_ne_add_F33_W36 :
    ¬ (2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W36.p) ∧ F33.bias ≤ W36.bias ∧
    addVia W36 (dy F33 7 (-4)) (dy F33 5 0) ≠ add (dy F33 7 (-4)) (dy F33 5 0) ∧
    addVia W36 (dy F33 7 (-4)) (dy F33 5 0) = dy F33 6 0 ∧
    add (dy F33 7 (-4)) (dy F33 5 0) = dy F33 5 0 := by …
```

Counterexample for `hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:266-269
theorem addVia_ne_add_F33_W29 :
    2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W29.p ∧ ¬ (F33.bias ≤ W29.bias) ∧ cvt F33 W29 (dy F33 4 0) = .inf false ∧
    addVia W29 (dy F33 4 0) (posZero F33) ≠ add (dy F33 4 0) (posZero F33) ∧
    addVia W29 (dy F33 4 0) (posZero F33) = .inf false ∧ add (dy F33 4 0) (posZero F33) = dy F33 4 0 := by …
```

**Non-vacuity witness.** Narrow (3,3) with wide (3,7) (p' = 2p + 1) and (3,8) (p' = 2p + 2): both hypotheses hold. (`doubleRounding_hyps_bounded`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:352-356
theorem doubleRounding_hyps_bounded :
    (2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W37.p ∧ F33.bias ≤ W37.bias) ∧ (2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W38.p ∧ F33.bias ≤ W38.bias) ∧
    (2 * F33.p ≤ W46.p ∧ 2 * F33.qneg ≤ W46.qneg ∧ F33.bias ≤ W46.bias) ∧
    (2 * F33.p ≤ W38.p ∧ 2 * F33.qneg ≤ W38.qneg ∧ F33.bias ≤ W38.bias) ∧
    (2 * F34.p ≤ W58.p ∧ 2 * F34.qneg ≤ W58.qneg ∧ F34.bias ≤ W58.bias) := by …
```

```lean
    ∀ x y : FP F33, addVia W37 x y = add x y := by …
```

```lean
    ∀ x y : FP F33, addVia W38 x y = add x y := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `double_rounding_add_innocuous`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- Samuel A. Figueroa, 'When is double rounding innocuous?', ACM SIGNUM Newsletter 30(3):21–26, July 1995, DOI 10.1145/221332.221334 (bibliographic data verified; the paper text was not accessible). Its theorems are restated in S. A. Figueroa del Cid, 'A Rigorous Framework for Fully Supporting the IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic in High-Level Programming Languages', PhD thesis, New York University, January 2000, §6.2, Theorems 1–5 and Table 6.1: for binary p-digit operands with round-to-nearest, rounding first to q digits is innocuous for addition iff q ≥ 2p + 1, for subtraction iff q ≥ 2p + 1 (ties to even), for multiplication (p ≥ 4) and division if q ≥ 2p, and for square root iff q ≥ 2p + 2; the proofs assume an unbounded exponent range.
- Pierre Roux, 'Innocuous Double Rounding of Basic Arithmetic Operations', Journal of Formalized Reasoning 7(1):131–142, 2014, DOI 10.6092/issn.1972-5787/4359. Coq proofs of the Figueroa bounds without underflow (Table I: +, −: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 1; ×, /: p2 ≥ 2p1; √: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 2), with optimality remarks. Table II adds conditions on emin under gradual underflow (e.g. ×: emin2 ≤ 2·emin1). Notation: Roux works in Flocq's FLT formats, where emin is the exponent of the least significant digit of the smallest subnormal (Flocq FLT.v: 'emin <= Fexp f' with an integer significand); in this package that is −qneg, not the IEEE normal minimum exponent 1 − bias. So Roux's 'emin2 ≤ 2·emin1' corresponds to 2·qneg f ≤ qneg g. Overflows are harmless whenever the narrow format is included in the wide one.

**Design question.** When is evaluating a narrow-format operation in a wider format and rounding back (x87 extended precision, FLT_EVAL_METHOD, emulation) identical to evaluating it directly?

#### 18. `double_rounding_mul_innocuous`

**Plain claim.** For all formats f (narrow, precision p) and g (wide, precision p'), if 2p ≤ p', 2·qneg f ≤ qneg g (the smallest subnormal of g is at most the square of that of f) and bias f ≤ bias g, then for all values x, y of f: mulVia g x y (convert x and y to g, multiply in g, convert the product back to f, each step correctly rounded with roundTiesToEven) equals mul x y.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / substantive / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.mulVia_eq_mul_of_two_mul_le_and_qneg_bias_le`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:526-527
theorem mulVia_eq_mul_of_two_mul_le_and_qneg_bias_le (f g : Fmt) (hp : 2 * f.p ≤ g.p)
    (hq : 2 * f.qneg ≤ g.qneg) (hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias) (x y : FP f) : mulVia g x y = mul x y := by …
```

**Proof outline.** Widening is exact (`cvt_up_fin`). The exact product of two magnitudes with `p` significant bits has at most `2p ≤ p'` significant bits, and under `2 * f.qneg ≤ g.qneg` it is an integer number of units of `g`; so the multiplication in `g` is exact, and converting back to `f` performs the single rounding of the direct product (`roundUnits_mul_pow_add` rescales it). If the exact product overflows in `g`, the direct product overflows in `f` (`overflow_transfer`). The argument is exactness of the wide product; there is no double rounding to analyse.

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown
- `hp : 2 * f.p ≤ g.p` — A product of two p-bit significands has at most 2p bits, so it is exact in g. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `mulVia_ne_mul_F34_W57` (witness class: the format pair f = (3,4), g = (5,7) (p' = 2p − 1, other hypotheses hold); in (3,3) via (4,5) no counterexample was found by search (not proved here)); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.
- `hq : 2 * f.qneg ≤ g.qneg` — Products in the narrow subnormal range must not be rounded on g's coarser subnormal grid (double rounding at underflow). **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W36` (witness class: the format pair f = (3,3), g = (3,6) (p' = 2p, same exponent range): (3/16)^2 gives +0 via g and 1/16 directly); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.
- `hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias` — Narrow values convert to g exactly and narrow overflow precedes wide overflow. **Tightness:** tight — counterexample `mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W29` (witness class: the format pair f = (3,3), g = (2,9): converting the input 4 to g already overflows to +∞, so 4 * 1 via g is +∞); the counterexample satisfies the other hypotheses and refutes the conclusion.

Counterexample for `hp : 2 * f.p ≤ g.p`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:275-279
theorem mulVia_ne_mul_F34_W57 :
    ¬ (2 * F34.p ≤ W57.p) ∧ 2 * F34.qneg ≤ W57.qneg ∧ F34.bias ≤ W57.bias ∧
    mulVia W57 (dy F34 13 (-5)) (dy F34 13 (-4)) ≠ mul (dy F34 13 (-5)) (dy F34 13 (-4)) ∧
    mulVia W57 (dy F34 13 (-5)) (dy F34 13 (-4)) = dy F34 5 (-4) ∧
    mul (dy F34 13 (-5)) (dy F34 13 (-4)) = dy F34 11 (-5) := by …
```

Counterexample for `hq : 2 * f.qneg ≤ g.qneg`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:285-289
theorem mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W36 :
    2 * F33.p ≤ W36.p ∧ ¬ (2 * F33.qneg ≤ W36.qneg) ∧ F33.bias ≤ W36.bias ∧
    mulVia W36 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 (-4)) ≠ mul (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 (-4)) ∧
    mulVia W36 (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 (-4)) = posZero F33 ∧
    mul (dy F33 3 (-4)) (dy F33 3 (-4)) = dy F33 1 (-4) := by …
```

Counterexample for `hb : f.bias ≤ g.bias`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:295-299
theorem mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W29 :
    2 * F33.p ≤ W29.p ∧ 2 * F33.qneg ≤ W29.qneg ∧ ¬ (F33.bias ≤ W29.bias) ∧
    cvt F33 W29 (dy F33 4 0) = .inf false ∧
    mulVia W29 (dy F33 4 0) (one F33) ≠ mul (dy F33 4 0) (one F33) ∧
    mulVia W29 (dy F33 4 0) (one F33) = .inf false ∧ mul (dy F33 4 0) (one F33) = dy F33 4 0 := by …
```

**Non-vacuity witness.** Narrow (3,3) with wide (4,6) and (3,8); narrow (3,4) with wide (5,8): all three hypotheses hold. (`doubleRounding_hyps_bounded`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:352-356
theorem doubleRounding_hyps_bounded :
    (2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W37.p ∧ F33.bias ≤ W37.bias) ∧ (2 * F33.p + 1 ≤ W38.p ∧ F33.bias ≤ W38.bias) ∧
    (2 * F33.p ≤ W46.p ∧ 2 * F33.qneg ≤ W46.qneg ∧ F33.bias ≤ W46.bias) ∧
    (2 * F33.p ≤ W38.p ∧ 2 * F33.qneg ≤ W38.qneg ∧ F33.bias ≤ W38.bias) ∧
    (2 * F34.p ≤ W58.p ∧ 2 * F34.qneg ≤ W58.qneg ∧ F34.bias ≤ W58.bias) := by …
```

```lean
    ∀ x y : FP F33, mulVia W46 x y = mul x y := by …
```

```lean
theorem mulVia_eq_mul_F33_W38 :
    ∀ x y : FP F33, mulVia W38 x y = mul x y := by …
```

```lean
theorem mulVia_eq_mul_F34_W58 :
    ∀ x y : FP F34, mulVia W58 x y = mul x y := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `double_rounding_mul_innocuous`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- Samuel A. Figueroa, 'When is double rounding innocuous?', ACM SIGNUM Newsletter 30(3):21–26, July 1995, DOI 10.1145/221332.221334 (bibliographic data verified; the paper text was not accessible). Its theorems are restated in S. A. Figueroa del Cid, 'A Rigorous Framework for Fully Supporting the IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic in High-Level Programming Languages', PhD thesis, New York University, January 2000, §6.2, Theorems 1–5 and Table 6.1: for binary p-digit operands with round-to-nearest, rounding first to q digits is innocuous for addition iff q ≥ 2p + 1, for subtraction iff q ≥ 2p + 1 (ties to even), for multiplication (p ≥ 4) and division if q ≥ 2p, and for square root iff q ≥ 2p + 2; the proofs assume an unbounded exponent range.
- Pierre Roux, 'Innocuous Double Rounding of Basic Arithmetic Operations', Journal of Formalized Reasoning 7(1):131–142, 2014, DOI 10.6092/issn.1972-5787/4359. Coq proofs of the Figueroa bounds without underflow (Table I: +, −: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 1; ×, /: p2 ≥ 2p1; √: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 2), with optimality remarks. Table II adds conditions on emin under gradual underflow (e.g. ×: emin2 ≤ 2·emin1). Notation: Roux works in Flocq's FLT formats, where emin is the exponent of the least significant digit of the smallest subnormal (Flocq FLT.v: 'emin <= Fexp f' with an integer significand); in this package that is −qneg, not the IEEE normal minimum exponent 1 − bias. So Roux's 'emin2 ≤ 2·emin1' corresponds to 2·qneg f ≤ qneg g. Overflows are harmless whenever the narrow format is included in the wide one.

**Design question.** When is evaluating a narrow-format operation in a wider format and rounding back (x87 extended precision, FLT_EVAL_METHOD, emulation) identical to evaluating it directly?

#### 19. `lt_not_ge_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b: the IEEE comparison a < b (lt) equals ¬(a >= b) if and only if neither a nor b is NaN.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / definitional / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.lt_eq_not_ge_iff`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:612-613
theorem lt_eq_not_ge_iff (a b : FP f) :
    lt a b = !(ge a b) ↔ (a.isNaN = false ∧ b.isNaN = false) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): NaN < 1 and NaN >= 1 are both false; 1 < 2 equals ¬(1 >= 2). (`lt_ne_not_ge_at_nan_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:306-307
theorem lt_ne_not_ge_at_nan_F33 :
    lt .nan (one F33) ≠ !(ge .nan (one F33)) ∧ lt (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) = !(ge (one F33) (dy F33 2 0)) := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `lt_not_ge_characterization`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019 §5.11 'Details of comparison predicates' (the standard's text was not accessible; quoted from a public verbatim quotation of the 754-2008 text and a late 754-2019 draft, Stack Overflow question 71224184, answer by E. Postpischil): 'Four mutually exclusive relations are possible: less than, equal, greater than, and unordered. The last case arises when at least one operand is NaN. Every NaN shall compare unordered with everything, including itself. Comparisons shall ignore the sign of zero (so +0 = −0).'
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.3 'Relational operators' (HTML rendering of N1570; the same text is F.8.3 in C99 N1256). 'x != x → false The expression x != x is true if x is a NaN.' 'x == x → true The expression x = x [sic; C99 N1256 F.8.3 reads 'The statement x == x'] is false if x is a NaN.' 'The sense of relational operators shall be maintained. This includes handling unordered cases as expressed by the source code.' EXAMPLE: 'if (a < b) f(); else g();' is not equivalent to 'if (a >= b) g(); else f();' (and the comments note that both raise 'invalid' if a and b are unordered) but is equivalent to 'if (!(a < b)) g(); else f();'.
- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint). Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › The IEEE Standard': 'Since x - x = +0 for all x, (+0) - (+0) = +0. However, -(+0) = -0, thus -x should not be defined as 0 - x', with footnote 27 'Unless the rounding mode is round toward -∞, in which case x - x = -0' (the sentence ignores NaN and infinities); the same paragraph: 'a NaN is never equal to any other number (including another NaN), so x = x is no longer always true' and 'NaNs are unordered with respect to all other numbers, so x ≤ y cannot be defined as not x > y'. Section 'The IEEE Standard › Special Quantities › Signed Zero': 'the IEEE standard defines comparison so that +0 = -0, rather than -0 < +0'.

**Design question.** Which comparison and min/max identities may an optimizer use on IEEE 754 values, and how do NaN and signed zero break them?

#### 20. `minLt_maxLt_commutativity_characterization`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b: minLt a b = minLt b a (with minLt a b = if a < b then a else b) holds if and only if it is not the case that exactly one of a, b is NaN or that {a, b} = {+0, −0}; the same holds for maxLt a b = if b < a then a else b.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T0 / shallow / proved

```lean
theorem minLt_maxLt_comm_iff (a b : FP f) :
    (minLt a b = minLt b a ↔ ¬ OneNaNOrOppositeZeros a b) ∧
    (maxLt a b = maxLt b a ↔ ¬ OneNaNOrOppositeZeros a b) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): minLt NaN 1 = 1 but minLt 1 NaN = NaN; minLt (+0) (−0) = −0 but minLt (−0) (+0) = +0; minLt 1 2 = minLt 2 1. (`minLt_maxLt_not_comm_F33`)


If the inputs compare equal (such as for the case of +0.0 and -0.0), either input may be returned non-deterministically'.
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.3 'Relational operators' (HTML rendering of N1570; the same text is F.8.3 in C99 N1256). 'x != x → false The expression x != x is true if x is a NaN.' 'x == x → true The expression x = x [sic; C99 N1256 F.8.3 reads 'The statement x == x'] is false if x is a NaN.' 'The sense of relational operators shall be maintained. This includes handling unordered cases as expressed by the source code.' EXAMPLE: 'if (a < b) f(); else g();' is not equivalent to 'if (a >= b) g(); else f();' (and the comments note that both raise 'invalid' if a and b are unordered) but is equivalent to 'if (!(a < b)) g(); else f();'.

**Design question.** Which comparison and min/max identities may an optimizer use on IEEE 754 values, and how do NaN and signed zero break them?

#### 21. `minimum_maximum_comm_assoc`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model, for all values a, b, c: the operations minimum and maximum modelled on IEEE 754-2019 §9.6 (NaN-propagating, with −0 below +0) are commutative and associative as identity of encodings.

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T0 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:704-707
theorem minimum_maximum_comm_assoc (a b c : FP f) :
    minimum a b = minimum b a ∧ maximum a b = maximum b a ∧
    minimum (minimum a b) c = minimum a (minimum b c) ∧
    maximum (maximum a b) c = maximum a (maximum b c) := …
```

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Prior art.**

- IEEE Std 754-2019 §9.6, quoted verbatim in WG21 paper P3008R6 (G. Brito Gadeschi and D. Sankel, 'Atomic floating-point min/max', 2025-06-19): 'minimum(x, y) is x if x < y, y if y < x, and a quiet NaN if either operand is a NaN, according to 6.2. For this operation, −0 compares less than +0'; 'minimumNumber(x, y) is x if x<y, y if y<x, and the number if one operand is a number and the other is a NaN'; these operations are recommended ('should'). P3008R6: 'IEEE 754-2019 removed minNum/maxNum'. C23 (as quoted in P3008R6): the fminimum_num functions 'determine the number if one argument is a number and the other is a NaN'.
- Rust core library, library/core/src/num/f32.rs at rust-lang/rust commit 268bdaabaceb7688b974bae0f96b9c5c1b6ab991. f32::minimum (lines 893–917; unstable, feature float_minimum_maximum): 'propagating NaN ... -0.0 is considered to be less than +0.0 ... This follows the IEEE 754-2019 semantics for minimum'.

No bridge between Toy.Float.FP and any of these is proved.

**Design question.** Which comparison and min/max identities may an optimizer use on IEEE 754 values, and how do NaN and signed zero break them?

#### 22. `flag_table_sufficiency`

**Plain claim.** In every format of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, one collapsed NaN), with ValidMod quantifying over the forms generated by Expr.variants: x + (−0) → x, x * 1 → x, x * (−1) → −x, x * 2 → x + x, (−0) − x → −x and x + y → y + x are valid without flags; x + (+0) → x, (+0) − x → −x and −(x − y) → y − x are ValidMod {nsz}; x − x → +0 is ValidMod {nnan}; x * (+0) → +0 is ValidMod {nnan, nsz}; (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) and (x * y) * z → x * (y * z) are ValidMod {reassoc}; x * y + z → fma x y z is ValidMod {contract}. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** theorem / T1 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.flagTable_sufficient`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Statements.lean:753-759
theorem flagTable_sufficient (f : Fmt) :
    Valid (rwAddNegZero f) ∧ ValidMod fNsz (rwAddPosZero f) ∧ Valid (rwMulOne f) ∧
    Valid (rwMulNegOne f) ∧ Valid (rwMulTwo f) ∧ ValidMod fNnan (rwSubSelf f) ∧
    ValidMod fNnanNsz (rwMulPosZero f) ∧ ValidMod fNsz (rwPosZeroSub f) ∧
    Valid (rwNegZeroSub f) ∧ ValidMod fNsz (rwNegSub f) ∧ Valid (rwAddComm f) ∧
    ValidMod fReassoc (rwAddAssoc f) ∧ ValidMod fReassoc (rwMulAssoc f) ∧
    ValidMod fContract (rwContract f) := by …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

**Non-vacuity witness.** Format (3,3): every row's source expression has an input admissible under all five flags (x = 1, y = 2, z = 1/4). (`flagTable_nonvacuous_F33`)

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Table.lean:150-165
theorem flagTable_nonvacuous_F33 :
    Admissible Flags.all (rwAddNegZero F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwAddPosZero F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulOne F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulNegOne F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulTwo F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwSubSelf F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulPosZero F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwPosZeroSub F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwNegZeroSub F33).lhs (env1 (one F33)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwNegSub F33).lhs (env2 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwAddComm F33).lhs (env2 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0)) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwAddAssoc F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulAssoc F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwContract F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwUncontract F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) := by …
```

**Non-claims.** Listed first under **Boundaries → Non-claims (per result)**, entry `flag_table_sufficiency`; read them before relying on this result.

**Prior art.**

- LLVM Language Reference Manual, llvm/docs/LangRef.md at llvm/llvm-project commit a2f1d1aeb6f5c700ac9a15592b6f81c3ec41e6c6, section 'Fast-Math Flags' (lines 4466–4566). nnan: 'Allow optimizations to assume the arguments and result are not NaN. If an argument is a nan, or the result would be a nan, it produces a poison value instead.' ninf: the same for +/-Inf. nsz: 'Unless otherwise mentioned, the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped.' The older wording, still in LLVM 20.1.0's LangRef.rst, was 'treat the sign of a zero argument or zero result as insignificant'. contract: 'Allow floating-point contraction (e.g., fusing a multiply followed by an addition into a fused multiply-and-add)'. reassoc: 'Allow algebraically equivalent transformations for floating-point instructions such as reassociation transformations. This may dramatically change results in floating-point.'
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.2 'Expression transformations' (HTML rendering of N1570). 'x - x → 0.0 The expressions x - x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN or infinite'. '0 * x → 0.0 The expressions 0*x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN, infinite, or -0' (this lists sufficient reasons; every negative finite x also gives −0). 'x + 0 → x The expressions x + 0 and x are not equivalent if x is -0, because (-0) + (+0) yields +0 (in the default rounding direction), not -0'. '-x ↔ 0 - x The expressions -x and 0 - x are not equivalent if x is +0, because -(+0) yields -0, but 0 - (+0) yields +0 (unless rounding is downward)'.
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.
- Heiko Becker, Eva Darulova, Magnus O. Myreen, Zachary Tatlock, 'Icing: Supporting Fast-Math Style Optimizations in a Verified Compiler', Computer Aided Verification (CAV 2019), LNCS 11562, pp. 155–173, Springer, 2019, DOI 10.1007/978-3-030-25543-5_10. Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.
- Sylvie Boldo, Jacques-Henri Jourdan, Xavier Leroy, Guillaume Melquiond, 'Verified Compilation of Floating-Point Computations', Journal of Automated Reasoning 54(2):135–163, 2015, DOI 10.1007/s10817-014-9317-x. Mechanized IEEE 754 semantics in CompCert (Coq/Flocq) with strict, value-preserving floating-point optimizations.

**Design question.** For each common floating-point peephole rewrite, which LLVM fast-math flags (nnan, ninf, nsz, reassoc, contract) exactly license it, under round-to-nearest-even?

#### 23. `flag_table_exact_F33`

**Plain claim.** In format (3,3) of the toy model (roundTiesToEven, one collapsed NaN), with ValidMod quantifying over the forms generated by Expr.variants, for every flag set T: x + (−0) → x, x * 1 → x, x * (−1) → −x, x * 2 → x + x, (−0) − x → −x and x + y → y + x are ValidMod T; x + (+0) → x, (+0) − x → −x and −(x − y) → y − x are ValidMod T iff nsz ∈ T; x − x → +0 iff nnan ∈ T; x * (+0) → +0 iff nnan ∈ T and nsz ∈ T; (x + y) + z → x + (y + z) and (x * y) * z → x * (y * z) iff reassoc ∈ T; x * y + z → fma x y z iff contract ∈ T; and fma x y z → x * y + z is ValidMod T for no T. Here ValidMod S r means: on every input admissible for r.lhs under S (no NaN/infinite argument or result of any node, for nnan/ninf), r.rhs evaluates, up to Rel S (identity, or equality up to the sign of zero under nsz), to the value of some form of r.lhs generated by Expr.variants S (at every node, after choosing forms of the children: the node itself, plus one reassociation step at an add/mul node under reassoc, plus one contraction of a product into an add/sub node under contract).

**Kind / tier / depth / proof status.** characterization / T1 / shallow / proved

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check). Every theorem quoted in this card is kernel-checked; axioms and hashes are under **Evidence**. Connections to real systems are separate `derived (modeling bridge)` claims under **Findings → Modelling bridges**.

**Lean statement** (`Toy.Float.flagTable_exact_F33`):

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Table.lean:125-140
theorem flagTable_exact_F33 :
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwAddNegZero F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwAddPosZero F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fNsz T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwMulOne F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwMulNegOne F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwMulTwo F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwSubSelf F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fNnan T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwMulPosZero F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fNnanNsz T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwPosZeroSub F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fNsz T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwNegZeroSub F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwNegSub F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fNsz T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwAddComm F33) ↔ Flags.Sub {} T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwAddAssoc F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fReassoc T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwMulAssoc F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fReassoc T) ∧
    (∀ T, ValidMod T (rwContract F33) ↔ Flags.Sub fContract T) ∧
    (∀ T, ¬ ValidMod T (rwUncontract F33)) := …
```

**Hypothesis ledger.**

- `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)` — Excludes degenerate formats without normal numbers or without a fractional significand bit; all definitions assume them. They are fields of the structure Fmt, so no statement can drop them. **Tightness:** unknown

```lean
    Admissible Flags.all (rwMulAssoc F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
    Admissible Flags.all (rwContract F33).lhs (env3 (one F33) (dy F33 2 0) (dy F33 1 (-2))) ∧
```

**Prior art.**

- LLVM Language Reference Manual, llvm/docs/LangRef.md at llvm/llvm-project commit a2f1d1aeb6f5c700ac9a15592b6f81c3ec41e6c6, section 'Fast-Math Flags' (lines 4466–4566). nnan: 'Allow optimizations to assume the arguments and result are not NaN. If an argument is a nan, or the result would be a nan, it produces a poison value instead.' ninf: the same for +/-Inf. nsz: 'Unless otherwise mentioned, the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped.' The older wording, still in LLVM 20.1.0's LangRef.rst, was 'treat the sign of a zero argument or zero result as insignificant'. contract: 'Allow floating-point contraction (e.g., fusing a multiply followed by an addition into a fused multiply-and-add)'. reassoc: 'Allow algebraically equivalent transformations for floating-point instructions such as reassociation transformations. This may dramatically change results in floating-point.'
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), Annex F.9.2 'Expression transformations' (HTML rendering of N1570). 'x - x → 0.0 The expressions x - x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN or infinite'. '0 * x → 0.0 The expressions 0*x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN, infinite, or -0' (this lists sufficient reasons; every negative finite x also gives −0). 'x + 0 → x The expressions x + 0 and x are not equivalent if x is -0, because (-0) + (+0) yields +0 (in the default rounding direction), not -0'. '-x ↔ 0 - x The expressions -x and 0 - x are not equivalent if x is +0, because -(+0) yields -0, but 0 - (+0) yields +0 (unless rounding is downward)'.
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.
- Heiko Becker, Eva Darulova, Magnus O. Myreen, Zachary Tatlock, 'Icing: Supporting Fast-Math Style Optimizations in a Verified Compiler', Computer Aided Verification (CAV 2019), LNCS 11562, pp. 155–173, Springer, 2019, DOI 10.1007/978-3-030-25543-5_10. Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.
- Sylvie Boldo, Jacques-Henri Jourdan, Xavier Leroy, Guillaume Melquiond, 'Verified Compilation of Floating-Point Computations', Journal of Automated Reasoning 54(2):135–163, 2015, DOI 10.1007/s10817-014-9317-x. Mechanized IEEE 754 semantics in CompCert (Coq/Flocq) with strict, value-preserving floating-point optimizations.

**Design question.** For each common floating-point peephole rewrite, which LLVM fast-math flags (nnan, ninf, nsz, reassoc, contract) exactly license it, under round-to-nearest-even?

### Modelling bridges

Each item below connects the model to a real system. None of them is a theorem; each is a `derived (modeling bridge)` claim, with its reasoning stated. Model–system relation: none proved, so no item transfers a theorem of this package to the system it names.

- **IEEE 754.** `add`, `sub`, `mul` and `fma` follow IEEE 754 binary arithmetic under roundTiesToEven: correct rounding, overflow to ∞ at the `2^emax(2 − 2^-p)` threshold, the §6.3 sign rules, and NaN for invalid operations. They do not model NaN payloads or NaN signs, signaling NaNs, exceptions or status flags, other rounding directions, flush-to-zero, division, square root, or the bit-level `negate` operation of §5.5.1 as distinct from arithmetic (in the model `FP.neg` flips the sign bit and maps the single NaN to itself). The general statements quantify over all formats with `e, p ≥ 2`.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: the definitions quoted under **Model definitions** were compared clause by clause with the rules of IEEE 754 §4.3.1, §5.4.1 and §6.3 as quoted in the prior-art entries; the definition examples (for instance `ex_round_pos_F33`: `15 → +∞`, `14.5 → 14` in format (3,3)) check the overflow threshold and tie-breaking on concrete values.

- **LLVM fast-math flags.** `Admissible` mirrors poison semantics for `nnan`/`ninf` when every instruction of the source carries the flag. `Rel` with `nsz` (equality up to the sign of zero, `EqNsz`) reflects the older LangRef wording ("treat the sign of a zero argument or zero result as insignificant"). Relative to the current wording ("the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped"): on the modelled operations `{neg, +, −, *, fma}`, where flipping the sign of a ±0 input can change at most the sign of a ±0 result, `EqNsz` is at least as permissive as the input-flip reading, and it is strictly coarser where nonzero operands underflow to `−0`: `(−1/16) * (1/2) → +0` is `ValidMod {nsz}` in format (3,3) (`nsz_rel_identifies_underflow_sign_F33`), which the input-flip reading does not license because no input is a zero. On full LLVM IR, which also has `fdiv` (flipping `−0.0` to `+0.0` in `1.0 / (−0.0)` changes `−∞` to `+∞`, a difference `EqNsz` does not identify), `bitcast` and `copysign`, the two readings are incomparable (not proved here). Whether the two readings agree on the four `nsz` rows of the flag table is not proved here. `Expr.variants` gives `reassoc` and `contract` a one-step-per-node reading; LLVM's `reassoc` licenses more (for example `(x + y) - y → x` under `reassoc nsz`, which is not in the table for that reason), and `ValidMod` is not transitive (`reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33`). The flag-table characterizations in format (3,3) match the conditions LLVM InstructionSimplify uses for `fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0` and `fmul nnan nsz X, 0 ==> 0` at the pinned commit.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: the LangRef fast-math section and the InstructionSimplify folds at the pinned commits (prior-art entries) were compared with `Admissible`, `Rel` and the flag table; the permissiveness comparison on `{neg, +, −, *, fma}` is a case analysis on where a zero input can influence a result in these operations (a zero operand of `+` or `−`, or a zero addend of `fma`, leaves a nonzero result unchanged and can change the sign of a zero result; a zero factor of `*` or of `fma`'s product gives a zero product whose sign is the XOR of the factor signs), not a Lean theorem.

- **C.** `contract` with `fma` models C11's contracted expressions (§6.5 ¶8, Annex F.7: correctly rounded once). `FP_CONTRACT` permits contraction without requiring it, so a C program's result may be either form. C11 F.9.2 and F.9.3 list the non-equivalences used here; the model's comparisons do not raise `invalid`.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: comparison of `Expr.variants` (contraction branch) and `fma` with the quoted N1570 text.

- **Rust.** Rust's primitive float operations are specified as IEEE 754-2008 with roundTiesToEven, with no implicit contraction (RFC 3514; `f32` documentation). That is the flag-free semantics of this model, apart from NaN bit patterns. `f32::min`/`max` are not `minLt`/`maxLt`: they return the non-NaN operand and may return either zero. `f32::minimum`/`maximum` (unstable) follow IEEE 754-2019 `minimum`/`maximum`.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: comparison with the quoted documentation at the pinned `rust-lang/rust` and `rust-lang/rfcs` commits.

- **Lean's built-in floats.** Lean `v4.34.1` ships a parametric collapsed-NaN model `Init.Data.Float.Model` backing `Float` and `Float32`. `Toy.Float.FP` is an independent definition; no equivalence between the two is proved, so no theorem here transfers to `Float` or `Float32`.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: inspection of `Init/Data/Float/Model/Format/Basic.lean` in the pinned toolchain source.

- **Double rounding in practice.** binary32 addition and multiplication computed in binary64 and rounded back, one operation at a time, satisfy the hypotheses of `addVia_eq_add_of_two_mul_add_one_le_and_bias_le` and `mulVia_eq_mul_of_two_mul_le_and_qneg_bias_le` when read through the format parameters: binary32 is `(e, p) = (8, 24)` (`bias = 127`, `qneg = 149`) and binary64 is `(11, 53)` (`bias = 1023`, `qneg = 1074`), so `2·24 + 1 ≤ 53`, `2·149 ≤ 1074` and `127 ≤ 1023`. The theorems are about `addVia`/`mulVia` in the toy model; that a real binary64 unit computes `addVia`/`mulVia` of the binary32 operands (including its NaN behaviour) is the modelling assumption of this bridge and is not proved here.
  Basis: derived (modeling bridge). Reasoning: arithmetic on the definitions `Fmt.bias = 2^(e−1) − 1` and `Fmt.qneg = bias + p − 2` quoted under **Model definitions** and **Auxiliary declarations cited by name**; the identification of real binary32/binary64 arithmetic with `FP` is the unproved step.


`FP.bits`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:131-134
def bits : FP f → Nat
  | .nan => (2 ^ f.e - 1) * 2 ^ (f.p - 1) + 2 ^ (f.p - 2)
  | .inf s => (if s then 2 ^ (f.e + f.p - 1) else 0) + (2 ^ f.e - 1) * 2 ^ (f.p - 1)
  | .fin s E M => (if s then 2 ^ (f.e + f.p - 1) else 0) + E.val * 2 ^ (f.p - 1) + M.val
```

`FP.isFinite`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:115-117
def isFinite : FP f → Bool
  | .fin .. => true
  | _ => false
```

`zero`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:145-146
def zero (f : Fmt) (neg : Bool) : FP f :=
  .fin neg ⟨0, f.expCount_pos⟩ ⟨0, f.fracCount_pos⟩
```

`FP.neg`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:137-140
def neg : FP f → FP f
  | .nan => .nan
  | .inf s => .inf (!s)
  | .fin s E M => .fin (!s) E M
```

`Flags.Sub`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:379-381
def Sub (S T : Flags) : Prop :=
  (S.nnan → T.nnan) ∧ (S.ninf → T.ninf) ∧ (S.nsz → T.nsz) ∧
  (S.reassoc → T.reassoc) ∧ (S.contract → T.contract)
```

`Rewrite`:

`ofDyadic`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:200-202
def ofDyadic (f : Fmt) (neg : Bool) (m : Nat) (k : Int) : FP f :=
  if 0 ≤ k + f.qneg then round f neg (m * 2 ^ (k + f.qneg).toNat) 0
  else round f neg m (-(k + f.qneg)).toNat
```

`Fmt.qneg`:

Named values and helpers.

`posZero`:


`negZero`:


`one`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:205

```

`maxFinite`:

```lean

def maxFinite (f : Fmt) (neg : Bool) : FP f := mkFin f neg f.maxUnits
```

`sval`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:211
def sval (neg : Bool) (u : Nat) : Int := if neg then -(u : Int) else (u : Int)
```

`bitLenAux`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Defs.lean:156-158
def bitLenAux : Nat → Nat → Nat
  | 0, _ => 0
  | k + 1, n => if n = 0 then 0 else bitLenAux k (n / 2) + 1
```

Flags.

`Flags.all`:

```lean
def all : Flags := ⟨true, true, true, true, true⟩
```

`Flag`:

```lean
inductive Flag where
  | nnan | ninf | nsz | reassoc | contract
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr
```

`Flags.has`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Lemmas.lean:25-30
def has (S : Flags) : Flag → Bool
  | .nnan => S.nnan
  | .ninf => S.ninf
  | .nsz => S.nsz
  | .reassoc => S.reassoc
  | .contract => S.contract
```

`Flags.allBut`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Lemmas.lean:33-38
def allBut : Flag → Flags
  | .nnan => { all with nnan := false }
  | .ninf => { all with ninf := false }
  | .nsz => { all with nsz := false }
  | .reassoc => { all with reassoc := false }
  | .contract => { all with contract := false }
```
