```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:71
def env2 {f : Fmt} (a b : FP f) : Fin 2 → FP f := fun i => if i.val = 0 then a else b
```

`env3`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Rewrites.lean:72-73
def env3 {f : Fmt} (a b c : FP f) : Fin 3 → FP f :=
  fun i => if i.val = 0 then a else if i.val = 1 then b else c
```

`envAssocOverflow`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:97-98
def envAssocOverflow : Fin 3 → FP F33 :=
  env3 (maxFinite F33 false) (maxFinite F33 false) (maxFinite F33 true)
```

`rwAssoc4Step1`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:373-375
def rwAssoc4Step1 (f : Fmt) : Rewrite f 4 :=
  ⟨.add (.add (.add (.var 0) (.var 1)) (.var 2)) (.var 3),
   .add (.add (.var 0) (.var 1)) (.add (.var 2) (.var 3))⟩
```

`rwAssoc4Step2`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:378-380
def rwAssoc4Step2 (f : Fmt) : Rewrite f 4 :=
  ⟨.add (.add (.var 0) (.var 1)) (.add (.var 2) (.var 3)),
   .add (.var 0) (.add (.var 1) (.add (.var 2) (.var 3)))⟩
```

`rwAssoc4Comp`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:383-384
def rwAssoc4Comp (f : Fmt) : Rewrite f 4 :=
  ⟨(rwAssoc4Step1 f).lhs, (rwAssoc4Step2 f).rhs⟩
```

`env4`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:387-388
def env4 {f : Fmt} (w x y z : FP f) : Fin 4 → FP f :=
  fun i => if i.val = 0 then w else if i.val = 1 then x else if i.val = 2 then y else z
```

`envAssoc4Witness`:

```lean
-- Toy/Float/Counterexamples.lean:391-392
def envAssoc4Witness : Fin 4 → FP F33 :=
  env4 (dy F33 4 0) (dy F33 1 (-1)) (dy F33 1 (-4)) (dy F33 1 (-4))
```

## Boundaries

### Non-claims (per result)

Each list is the `non_claims` field of the result in `results.json`, verbatim.

#### `add_mul_fma_comm`

- NaN encoding: the model has one NaN, so '=' means identical encodings modulo NaN payload and NaN sign. Real hardware and LLVM choose the payload and sign of a NaN result non-deterministically (LLVM LangRef 'Behavior of Floating-Point NaN values'), and signaling NaNs are not modelled (not proved here). So a + b and b + a may differ in NaN bits on such systems (not proved here).
- It is not associativity: add is not associative (add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_of_prec_le_two_bias, add_assoc_fails_by_overflow), and mul is not associative in format (3,3) (rwMulAssoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33).
- The fma conjunct swaps the two multiplicands; it says nothing about the addend: symmetry of fma in its addend is not claimed, and fma x y z and fma x z y differ in general (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_negZero_mul_one_identity`

- NaN encoding: the model has one NaN, so '=' means identical encodings modulo NaN payload and NaN sign. Real hardware and LLVM choose the payload and sign of a NaN result non-deterministically (LLVM LangRef 'Behavior of Floating-Point NaN values'), and signaling NaNs are not modelled (not proved here). On hardware, NaN * 1 may change a NaN payload or quiet a signaling NaN (not proved here).
- The law x + (+0) = x is different and fails at x = −0 (add_posZero_right_eq_iff_and_eqNsz).
- Rounding attribute: the model fixes roundTiesToEven; under IEEE 754 roundTowardNegative the sign of an exact zero sum changes ((+0) + (−0) = −0 and x − x = −0 for finite x) (not proved here; other rounding attributes are not modelled).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_posZero_identity_characterization`

- The equivalence with x ≠ −0 is about identity of encodings in the toy model; NaN payload effects are not modelled.
- Rounding attribute: the model fixes roundTiesToEven; under IEEE 754 roundTowardNegative the sign of an exact zero sum changes ((+0) + (−0) = −0 and x − x = −0 for finite x) (not proved here; other rounding attributes are not modelled).
- That {nsz} is exactly the family of flag sets licensing x + (+0) → x is the table result rwAddPosZero_validMod_iff_F33, proved in format (3,3). For every format, sufficiency of {nsz} is flagTable_sufficient; necessity of nsz is proved in format (3,3), and in other formats it is neither established nor refuted here.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `sub_self_characterization`

- It does not say that x − x → +0 needs both nnan and ninf: under LLVM's nnan, which also excludes NaN results, the second conjunct shows that excluding NaN results already excludes x = ±∞. Sufficiency of {nnan} in every format is flagTable_sufficient; that {nnan} is exactly the required family is rwSubSelf_validMod_iff_F33, proved in format (3,3); in other formats it is neither established nor refuted here.
- Under a reading of 'no NaNs' that restricts the inputs but not the results, the input +∞ would not be excluded; the package does not model that reading as a flag.
- Rounding attribute: the model fixes roundTiesToEven; under IEEE 754 roundTowardNegative the sign of an exact zero sum changes ((+0) + (−0) = −0 and x − x = −0 for finite x) (not proved here; other rounding attributes are not modelled).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `feq_self_characterization`

- == is the IEEE quiet equality feq; identity of encodings (=) is a different relation, under which x = x always holds and +0 and −0 differ.
- Comparison rewrites are not rows of the flag table, because the expression language is floating-point valued; the statement that x == x → true is licensed by an assumption that x is not NaN is prose derived from this characterization.
- feq returns a Bool and models no exception flags (IEEE 754 quiet versus signaling predicates are not distinguished).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `mul_posZero_characterization`

- Sufficiency of {nnan, nsz} for x * (+0) → +0 holds in every format (flagTable_sufficient); that ninf is not needed and that {nnan, nsz} is exactly the required family is rwMulPosZero_validMod_iff_F33, proved in format (3,3); in other formats it is neither established nor refuted here.
- C11 F.9.2 lists 'NaN, infinite, or −0' as reasons for non-equivalence; this characterization adds every negative finite x. It does not contradict C11's table, which gives sufficient reasons.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_assoc_fails_by_rounding`

- The hypothesis h is tight in the sense of this ledger for the exposed witnesses assocRoundA, assocRoundB (counterexample in format (2,2), add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_hyp_tight_F22); for other witnesses its tightness is neither established nor refuted here. It is not necessary for the existence of an all-normal counterexample: format (2,3) violates h and still has one (add_assoc_rounding_all_normal_F23).
- It does not show that associativity fails for every triple, or for most triples.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_assoc_fails_by_overflow`

- This failure needs an infinite intermediate result, so it is excluded by ninf (add_assoc_overflow_excluded_by_ninf_F33, format (3,3)); rounding counterexamples are not excluded (add_assoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33).
- Overflow of M + M to +∞ relies on roundTiesToEven; under roundTowardZero or roundTowardNegative, M + M rounds to M instead (not proved here; other rounding attributes are not modelled).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_assoc_zero_sign_impossible`

- It does not say signed zeros are harmless for re-bracketing in general: the nearby re-bracketing (a − b) − c versus a − (b + c) differs only in zero sign at (−0, +0, −0) (sub_rebracket_differs_only_in_zero_sign_F33), and so does −(x − y) versus y − x (rwNegSub_validMod_iff_F33). It covers pure add re-bracketing; it neither establishes nor refutes zero-sign agreement for expressions mixing sub, mul or fma.
- Escape hatch (subnormal handling): the proof uses gradual underflow, under which every nonzero exact sum of format values is at least one subnormal unit 2^−qneg in magnitude and so never rounds to ±0. Under flush-to-zero (abrupt underflow preserving the sign), all-normal triples can give −0 in one bracketing and +0 in the other, e.g. (−2.5, 2.0, 0.375) in format (3,3) (not proved here; flush-to-zero is not modelled).
- Escape hatch (rounding attribute): the proof uses the roundTiesToEven sum-sign rule; other rounding attributes are not modelled (not proved here).
- It does not claim novelty; it is a direct consequence of the IEEE 754 §6.3 sum-sign rule and gradual underflow. No explicit statement of it was located in the sources checked.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `add_assoc_survives_restriction_flags`

- It is proved in format (3,3) for this input; it does not say how often reassociation changes results. In every format, overflow inputs also break associativity (add_assoc_fails_by_overflow), but those are excluded by ninf (add_assoc_overflow_excluded_by_ninf_F33).
- ValidMod quantifies over the one-step-per-node forms generated by Expr.variants, not over every algebraically equivalent form; it is not transitive: the two one-step reassociations ((w + x) + y) + z → (w + x) + (y + z) and (w + x) + (y + z) → w + (x + (y + z)) are each valid modulo {reassoc}, but their composite is not valid even modulo all five flags (reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33; Expr.variants does not generate w + (x + (y + z)) from ((w + x) + y) + z, reassoc_variants_omits_right_assoc_F33). Rewrites such as (x + y) − y → x, which LLVM performs under reassoc nsz, are outside this permission model.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `permissions_refine_iff_laws`

- The pointwise equivalences are definitional unfoldings of Expr.variants for these two source expressions (the generated forms are the source itself and x + (y + z), respectively fma x y z); they contain no floating-point reasoning. The floating-point content is in the exposed witnesses.
- The every-format witnesses use overflow, so they are not admissible under ninf; a finite all-normal witness for reassoc is add_assoc_not_validMod_allBut_reassoc_F33 and one for contract is fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33, both proved in format (3,3); finite all-normal witnesses in other formats are neither established nor refuted here.
- The corollary not_permittedFormsAgree (neither permission is a refinement of the flag-free semantics, in every format) follows from the witnesses; it does not quantify over larger expressions or other generators.
- ValidMod quantifies over the one-step-per-node forms generated by Expr.variants, not over every algebraically equivalent form; it is not transitive: the two one-step reassociations ((w + x) + y) + z → (w + x) + (y + z) and (w + x) + (y + z) → w + (x + (y + z)) are each valid modulo {reassoc}, but their composite is not valid even modulo all five flags (reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33; Expr.variants does not generate w + (x + (y + z)) from ((w + x) + y) + z, reassoc_variants_omits_right_assoc_F33). Rewrites such as (x + y) − y → x, which LLVM performs under reassoc nsz, are outside this permission model.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal`

- Proved for one instance in format (3,3); no claim about how often contraction changes results.
- Here the fused result is the more accurate one (error 29/128 versus 35/128, nv_fma_accuracy_F33). That is not always so: if the fused result overflows, it is not (fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `fma_nearest`

- Finiteness of a, b, c is not assumed because it follows from h (fma_isFinite_imp, proved).
- fma is, by definition, the RNE rounding of the exact value when the exact a·b + c is nonzero and a, b, c are finite (fma_eq_round_exactMulAdd, proved, with the guard exactMulAdd a b c ≠ 0; an exactly zero result is signed by the sum rule instead). This theorem is the nearest property of that rounding.
- It concerns absolute error measured exactly; it does not claim the fused result is the unique nearest value (ties exist). errOf assigns error 0 to both +0 and −0, so the theorem constrains magnitude distance, not the sign of a zero result (which IEEE 754 §6.3 governs separately).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `fma_errLe_twoStep`

- Derivation: fma_errLe_twoStep_of_fma_finite is fma_nearest_of_fma_finite instantiated at r := a * b + c; it adds nothing beyond that instance.
- It does not say contraction preserves results: fma and a * b + c differ (fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33), and contraction can turn a*b − a*b from +0 into a nonzero value (contract_mulSubMul_nonzero_F33).
- 'More accurate' is about a single expression a*b + c; it says nothing about accuracy of larger expressions after contraction.
- The overflow corner is exhibited in format (3,3); whether it occurs in formats (2,2) or (3,2) is not stated (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `contraction_hazard_mulSubMul`

- An inexact product does not always yield a nonzero residual: at (1/16, 3.5) in format (3,3) the product rounds to the normal 1/4 but fma a b (−(a*b)) is −0 (fma_mulSubMul_zero_for_inexact_product_F33).
- The residual can be negative: when the product rounds up, fma a b (−(a*b)) < 0, e.g. −1/8 at (5/4, 3/2) in format (3,3) (fma_mulSubMul_negative_F33); downstream operations with restricted domains (such as a square root of x*x − x*x) could then produce NaN (not proved here; square root is not modelled).
- ValidMod quantifies over the one-step-per-node forms generated by Expr.variants, not over every algebraically equivalent form; it is not transitive: the two one-step reassociations ((w + x) + y) + z → (w + x) + (y + z) and (w + x) + (y + z) → w + (x + (y + z)) are each valid modulo {reassoc}, but their composite is not valid even modulo all five flags (reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33; Expr.variants does not generate w + (x + (y + z)) from ((w + x) + y) + z, reassoc_variants_omits_right_assoc_F33). Rewrites such as (x + y) − y → x, which LLVM performs under reassoc nsz, are outside this permission model.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `double_rounding_not_innocuous`

- This is an introductory witness with p' = p + 1. The sharp witness for addition, with p' = 2p and the exponent-range hypothesis satisfied, is addVia_ne_add_F33_W36 (the tightness counterexample of double_rounding_add_innocuous).
- One instance in one format pair; no claim about frequency.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `double_rounding_add_innocuous`

- Subtraction is not stated separately; sub is add with a negated operand, but no theorem about a subVia is given.
- These theorems concern one operation at a time: each addition (addVia) or multiplication (mulVia) is widened to g and rounded back to f immediately. They do not license evaluating a multi-operation expression such as (a + b) + c or a * b + c in g with a single final rounding to f (as with x87 extended registers or C FLT_EVAL_METHOD 1 or 2), which can change results (not proved here; compare fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33 for the single-rounding effect).
- Neither theorem covers division, square root or fma: whether a narrow fma can be emulated by a wide fma rounded back to f under these precision bounds is not stated (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `double_rounding_mul_innocuous`

- The hypothesis hq is stated with qneg (the exponent of the smallest subnormal, negated), not with the IEEE normal minimum exponent: with emin = 1 − bias, the inequality emin_g ≤ 2·emin_f fails for f = (3,3), g = (3,8), whose instance is nevertheless proved (mulVia_eq_mul_F33_W38).
- These theorems concern one operation at a time: each addition (addVia) or multiplication (mulVia) is widened to g and rounded back to f immediately. They do not license evaluating a multi-operation expression such as (a + b) + c or a * b + c in g with a single final rounding to f (as with x87 extended registers or C FLT_EVAL_METHOD 1 or 2), which can change results (not proved here; compare fma_ne_twoStep_all_normal_F33 for the single-rounding effect).
- Neither theorem covers division, square root or fma: whether a narrow fma can be emulated by a wide fma rounded back to f under these precision bounds is not stated (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `lt_not_ge_characterization`

- It concerns quiet comparisons returning Bool on the toy model's values; it is not a statement about branch structure or about the order key itself.
- lt, le, ge and feq return Bool and model no exception flags. In IEEE 754 §5.11 and in C with FENV_ACCESS ON (Annex F.9.3), the relational predicates <, <=, >, >= signal the invalid exception on any NaN operand, including a quiet NaN, so rewriting between them also changes exception behaviour (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `minLt_maxLt_commutativity_characterization`

- minLt and maxLt are not associative in the presence of NaN either: minLt (minLt 1 NaN) 2 = 2 while minLt 1 (minLt NaN 2) = 1 (minLt_not_assoc_at_nan_F33, format (3,3)).
- minLt is not Rust's f32::min (which returns the non-NaN argument and may return either zero) nor IEEE 754-2019 minimum/minimumNumber; these are prose bridges, not modelled definitions.
- lt, le, ge and feq return Bool and model no exception flags. In IEEE 754 §5.11 and in C with FENV_ACCESS ON (Annex F.9.3), the relational predicates <, <=, >, >= signal the invalid exception on any NaN operand, including a quiet NaN, so rewriting between them also changes exception behaviour (not proved here).
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `minimum_maximum_comm_assoc`

- NaN encoding: the model has one NaN, so '=' means identical encodings modulo NaN payload and NaN sign. Real hardware and LLVM choose the payload and sign of a NaN result non-deterministically (LLVM LangRef 'Behavior of Floating-Point NaN values'), and signaling NaNs are not modelled (not proved here). With several NaN operands, real implementations may return different NaN encodings depending on order (not proved here).
- minimum and maximum model IEEE 754-2019's NaN-propagating operations (C23 fminimum/fmaximum); they are not the number-preferring minimumNumber/maximumNumber (C23 fminimum_num/fmaximum_num, which return the number when one operand is NaN) and not the deleted IEEE 754-2008 minNum/maxNum.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `flag_table_sufficiency`

- This result states sufficiency of each flag set, in every format. That each flag set is also necessary (minimal) is flagTable_exact_F33, proved in format (3,3); in other formats minimality is neither established nor refuted here.
- Escape hatches of the flag-free and {nnan} rows: all rows assume roundTiesToEven, gradual underflow, no exception status flags or traps, and one signless NaN. Under roundTowardNegative, x + (−0) → x fails at x = +0, (−0) − x → −x fails at x = −0, and x − x → +0 needs {nnan, nsz} rather than {nnan}; on IEEE 754 hardware and in Rust (RFC 3514), unary −x flips the NaN sign bit deterministically and does not quiet a signaling NaN, whereas x * (−1) and (−0) − x are arithmetic operations that may quiet signaling NaNs and need not flip NaN signs (not proved here; none of this is modelled).
- Rel models nsz as equality of results up to the sign of zero (EqNsz). On the modelled operations {neg, +, −, *, fma}, where flipping the sign of a ±0 input can change at most the sign of a ±0 result, EqNsz is at least as permissive as LLVM's current input-zero-flip wording ('the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped'), and it is strictly coarser on expressions whose nonzero operands underflow to −0: the closed rewrite (−1/16) * (1/2) → +0 is ValidMod {nsz} in format (3,3) although no operand is a zero (nsz_rel_identifies_underflow_sign_F33). This comparison is a derived modelling bridge, not a theorem. On full LLVM IR, which also has fdiv (where flipping −0.0 to +0.0 in 1.0 / (−0.0) changes −∞ to +∞), bitcast and copysign, the two readings are incomparable (not proved here). Whether they agree on the four nsz rows of the table is not proved here.
- ValidMod quantifies over the one-step-per-node forms generated by Expr.variants, not over every algebraically equivalent form; it is not transitive: the two one-step reassociations ((w + x) + y) + z → (w + x) + (y + z) and (w + x) + (y + z) → w + (x + (y + z)) are each valid modulo {reassoc}, but their composite is not valid even modulo all five flags (reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33; Expr.variants does not generate w + (x + (y + z)) from ((w + x) + y) + z, reassoc_variants_omits_right_assoc_F33). Rewrites such as (x + y) − y → x, which LLVM performs under reassoc nsz, are outside this permission model.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

#### `flag_table_exact_F33`

- The necessity (minimality) directions are proved in format (3,3); in other formats they are neither established nor refuted here (the sufficiency directions hold in every format, flagTable_sufficient).
- fma x y z → x * y + z is not ValidMod for any flag set in format (3,3) (rwUncontract_never_validMod_F33) because Expr.variants has no rewrite rules at fma nodes; the permission model has no 'uncontract'.
- Escape hatches of the flag-free and {nnan} rows: all rows assume roundTiesToEven, gradual underflow, no exception status flags or traps, and one signless NaN. Under roundTowardNegative, x + (−0) → x fails at x = +0, (−0) − x → −x fails at x = −0, and x − x → +0 needs {nnan, nsz} rather than {nnan}; on IEEE 754 hardware and in Rust (RFC 3514), unary −x flips the NaN sign bit deterministically and does not quiet a signaling NaN, whereas x * (−1) and (−0) − x are arithmetic operations that may quiet signaling NaNs and need not flip NaN signs (not proved here; none of this is modelled).
- Rel models nsz as equality of results up to the sign of zero (EqNsz). On the modelled operations {neg, +, −, *, fma}, where flipping the sign of a ±0 input can change at most the sign of a ±0 result, EqNsz is at least as permissive as LLVM's current input-zero-flip wording ('the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped'), and it is strictly coarser on expressions whose nonzero operands underflow to −0: the closed rewrite (−1/16) * (1/2) → +0 is ValidMod {nsz} in format (3,3) although no operand is a zero (nsz_rel_identifies_underflow_sign_F33). This comparison is a derived modelling bridge, not a theorem. On full LLVM IR, which also has fdiv (where flipping −0.0 to +0.0 in 1.0 / (−0.0) changes −∞ to +∞), bitcast and copysign, the two readings are incomparable (not proved here). Whether they agree on the four nsz rows of the table is not proved here.
- ValidMod quantifies over the one-step-per-node forms generated by Expr.variants, not over every algebraically equivalent form; it is not transitive: the two one-step reassociations ((w + x) + y) + z → (w + x) + (y + z) and (w + x) + (y + z) → w + (x + (y + z)) are each valid modulo {reassoc}, but their composite is not valid even modulo all five flags (reassoc_validMod_not_transitive_F33; Expr.variants does not generate w + (x + (y + z)) from ((w + x) + y) + z, reassoc_variants_omits_right_assoc_F33). Rewrites such as (x + y) − y → x, which LLVM performs under reassoc nsz, are outside this permission model.
- Model–system relation: none proved. This is a statement about the toy model Toy.Float (IEEE binary fields; roundTiesToEven as the sole rounding mode; gradual underflow; no exception status flags or traps; a single NaN with payload and sign collapsed). It neither establishes nor refutes the corresponding claims about IEEE 754 hardware, C, LLVM, Rust, or Lean's built-in Float, Float32 and Init.Data.Float.Model; no representation-equivalence proof to any of them is given.

### Escape hatches

- `add_assoc_zero_sign_impossible` (`add_assoc_defect_never_only_zero_sign`) depends on gradual underflow and on the roundTiesToEven sum-sign rule. Under flush-to-zero, or under roundTowardNegative (where an exactly zero sum of opposite-signed operands is `−0`), the impossibility is not established; the model has neither, and an all-normal flush-to-zero triple `(−2.5, 2.0, 0.375)` in format (3,3) is recorded as a hint (not proved here). The positive control `sub_rebracket_differs_only_in_zero_sign_F33` shows that a nearby re-bracketing involving subtraction does differ in the sign of zero alone.
- The double-rounding theorems are defeated by dropping any one hypothesis, within the recorded witness classes: `addVia_ne_add_F33_W36` (`p' = 2p`), `addVia_ne_add_F33_W29` (`bias f > bias g`), `mulVia_ne_mul_F34_W57` (`p' = 2p − 1`), `mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W36` (`2·qneg f > qneg g`), `mulVia_ne_mul_F33_W29` (`bias f > bias g`).
- The accuracy theorems `fma_nearest_of_fma_finite` and `fma_errLe_twoStep_of_fma_finite` are defeated by dropping the finiteness of the fused result (`fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33`).

### Hypotheses with `unknown` tightness

In every result the format invariants `2 ≤ e` and `2 ≤ p` are fields of the structure `Fmt`, so no statement can drop them; their tightness is recorded as `unknown`:

- `add_mul_fma_comm`: `format invariants 2 ≤ e, 2 ≤ p (fields of Fmt)`

### Stronger or adjacent statements not established here

- Multi-operation evaluation in a wider format with a single final rounding (x87 extended registers, C `FLT_EVAL_METHOD` 1 or 2): the double-rounding theorems concern one operation at a time (not proved here).
- Double rounding for subtraction as a separate `subVia`, for division, square root or `fma`: not stated (not proved here).
- Uniqueness of the nearest value in `fma_nearest_of_fma_finite`: ties exist; the theorem constrains error magnitude, not which nearest value or which zero sign is returned.
- Any statement about NaN payloads, NaN signs, signaling NaNs, exception flags, other rounding directions or flush-to-zero.
- Agreement of `EqNsz` with LLVM's current `nsz` wording on the four `nsz` rows of the flag table, and any comparison on full LLVM IR (not proved here).
- Necessity (minimality) of the flag sets outside format (3,3): neither established nor refuted here.

### Disproved or rejected candidate statements

No result of this package was dropped or found false; all 23 results are proved as stated. Earlier formulations of several of these claims were false or imprecise; each was replaced by the statement given here, and the kernel-checked theorem that settles it is cited:

- "`x − x → +0.0` is valid modulo `nnan ∧ ninf`" is true but not minimal: `{nnan}` suffices, and in format (3,3) exactly the flag sets containing `nnan` license it (`rwSubSelf_validMod_iff_F33`; sufficiency in every format, `flagTable_sufficient`).
- "`x * 0.0 → 0.0` needs `ninf`": in format (3,3) exactly the flag sets containing `nnan` and `nsz` license it, so `ninf` is not needed there (`rwMulPosZero_validMod_iff_F33`); in every format `{nnan, nsz}` suffices (`flagTable_sufficient`).
- "Associativity of addition can fail through the sign of zero alone": false under roundTiesToEven with gradual underflow (`add_assoc_defect_never_only_zero_sign`).
- "`fma` is at most as inaccurate as the two-step `a * b + c` whenever the two-step form does not overflow": false (`fma_overflows_while_twoStep_finite_F33`); the corrected hypothesis is finiteness of the fused result.
- The uniform textbook bound `p' ≥ 2p + 2` (the square-root bound, which also suffices for addition and multiplication under an unbounded exponent range) is not the per-operation statement; it was replaced by the bounds `2p + 1` (addition) and `2p` (multiplication), each with exponent-range hypotheses whose tightness counterexamples are listed under **Escape hatches**.
- A global (all-inputs) form of `permittedFormsAgreeAt_iff_laws` was vacuously true, because both sides of both equivalences are false in every format (`not_permittedFormsAgree`, `reassoc_contract_change_result_at_maxFinite`); it was replaced by the pointwise statement.
- "`fma(a, b, −(a*b))` is nonzero whenever `a*b` is normal and inexact": false (`fma_mulSubMul_zero_for_inexact_product_F33`).
- An all-normal associativity witness without a range hypothesis: the exposed witness needs `p + 1 ≤ 2·bias` (`add_assoc_fails_by_rounding_all_normal_hyp_tight_F22`), although format (2,3) has a different all-normal witness (`add_assoc_rounding_all_normal_F23`).

## Evidence

### Kernel-check record

Basis: execution (Lean kernel check).

- Toolchain: `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1` (tag commit `5045d0056413266e57c625dcd7c365b10e377c52`, fetched from the GitHub API), no Mathlib, no Lake dependencies. `lake build` of the library `Toy` succeeds with no `sorry` warnings; no Lean source contains `sorry`, `admit`, `native_decide`, `axiom`, `opaque`, `implemented_by`, `extern` or `unsafe`.
- Axioms (`Lean.collectAxioms`, recorded in `audit/axioms.json`) over all 805 non-internal declarations of the library: 325 use Quot.sound, propext; 287 use no axioms; 111 use propext; 82 use Classical.choice, Quot.sound, propext. Every set is a subset of `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`.
- Statement and definition-closure hashes (`audit/statements.json`, 241 names): SHA-256 of the Lean `repr` of each declaration's type, and SHA-256 of its definition closure (the sorted, newline-joined list, over every package-local constant reachable from the statement, of the constant's name, the `repr` of its type, and, for non-theorems, the `repr` of its value; theorem bodies are excluded). Every result, counterexample, non-vacuity witness and positive control named in `results.json` has a recorded hash, and so does every package-local declaration (definition, structure, inductive type, theorem or lemma) that this report names in prose, in tables or inside a quoted Lean block; constructors and structure fields are covered by the entry of their type (`FP`, `Expr`, `Flag`, `Flags`, `Fmt`, `Rewrite`). Every such declaration is also quoted verbatim in this report.
- The **Used by** column gives, first, the role a declaration plays in a result card (`result`, `non-vacuity` witness, `positive control`, or `counterexample for` a named hypothesis), and then the parts of this report that cite it (`cited in`: the summary, the model definitions, a result card together with its non-claims, the modelling bridges, the auxiliary or helper quotes, or the boundaries). Shared model definitions and definition examples have no card role. Which card's `lean_names` list in `results.json` records a name only determines where its hash is listed; it does not mean that the card depends on it.

### Primary sources (prior art)

Each source is listed once, and the clauses or passages relied on are grouped under it, with what was read and what was not accessible. The same entries, split by result, are the prior-art paragraphs of the cards.

- IEEE Std 754-2019, IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic, IEEE, July 2019, DOI 10.1109/IEEESTD.2019.8766229. The 2019 text was not accessible; each clause below is quoted from the public source named with it.
  - §4.3.1, quoted in public sources: under roundTiesToEven 'an infinitely precise result with magnitude at least b^emax(b − ½b^(1−p)) shall round to ∞ with no change in sign'.
  - IEEE Std 754-2008 §5.4.1, quoted verbatim in a public source: fusedMultiplyAdd(x, y, z) 'computes (x×y)+z as if with unbounded range and precision, rounding only once to the destination format ... and so fusedMultiplyAdd differs from a multiplication operation followed by an addition operation'. The 2019 wording and section number were not checked at a 2019 source.
  - §5.11 'Details of comparison predicates', quoted from a public verbatim quotation of the 754-2008 text and a late 754-2019 draft (Stack Overflow question 71224184, answer by E. Postpischil): 'Four mutually exclusive relations are possible: less than, equal, greater than, and unordered. The last case arises when at least one operand is NaN. Every NaN shall compare unordered with everything, including itself. Comparisons shall ignore the sign of zero (so +0 = −0).'
  - §6.3 'The sign bit', quoted from public verbatim quotations of the IEEE 754-2008 §6.3 text: an exactly zero sum of operands with opposite signs is +0 in every rounding-direction attribute except roundTowardNegative, and 'x + x = x − (−x) retains the same sign as x even when x is zero'; the sign of a product is the exclusive OR of the operand signs, also when the result is zero; for fusedMultiplyAdd an exactly zero (a×b)+c is signed by the sum rule, and a nonzero exact result that rounds to zero takes the sign of the exact result. §6.3 concerns non-comparison operations.
  - §9.6, quoted verbatim in WG21 paper P3008R6 (G. Brito Gadeschi and D. Sankel, 'Atomic floating-point min/max', 2025-06-19): 'minimum(x, y) is x if x < y, y if y < x, and a quiet NaN if either operand is a NaN, according to 6.2. For this operation, −0 compares less than +0'; 'minimumNumber(x, y) is x if x<y, y if y<x, and the number if one operand is a number and the other is a NaN'; these operations are recommended ('should'). P3008R6: 'IEEE 754-2019 removed minNum/maxNum'. C23 (as quoted in P3008R6): the fminimum_num functions 'determine the number if one argument is a number and the other is a NaN'.
- David Goldberg, 'What every computer scientist should know about floating-point arithmetic', ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5–48, March 1991, DOI 10.1145/103162.103163 (quoted from the edited reprint, Appendix D of Sun's Numerical Computation Guide; section paths are those of the reprint).
  - Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › Ambiguity': 'Due to roundoff errors, the associative laws of algebra do not necessarily hold for floating-point numbers', with (x+y)+z = 1 but x+(y+z) = 0 for x = 10^30, y = −10^30, z = 1.
  - Section 'Systems Aspects › Languages and Compilers › The IEEE Standard': 'Since x - x = +0 for all x, (+0) - (+0) = +0. However, -(+0) = -0, thus -x should not be defined as 0 - x', with footnote 27 'Unless the rounding mode is round toward -∞, in which case x - x = -0' (the sentence ignores NaN and infinities); the same paragraph: 'a NaN is never equal to any other number (including another NaN), so x = x is no longer always true' and 'NaNs are unordered with respect to all other numbers, so x ≤ y cannot be defined as not x > y'. Section 'The IEEE Standard › Special Quantities › Signed Zero': 'the IEEE standard defines comparison so that +0 = -0, rather than -0 < +0'.
- Samuel A. Figueroa, 'When is double rounding innocuous?', ACM SIGNUM Newsletter 30(3):21–26, July 1995, DOI 10.1145/221332.221334 (bibliographic data verified; the paper text was not accessible). Its theorems are restated in S. A. Figueroa del Cid, 'A Rigorous Framework for Fully Supporting the IEEE Standard for Floating-Point Arithmetic in High-Level Programming Languages', PhD thesis, New York University, January 2000, §6.2, Theorems 1–5 and Table 6.1: for binary p-digit operands with round-to-nearest, rounding first to q digits is innocuous for addition iff q ≥ 2p + 1, for subtraction iff q ≥ 2p + 1 (ties to even), for multiplication (p ≥ 4) and division if q ≥ 2p, and for square root iff q ≥ 2p + 2; the proofs assume an unbounded exponent range.
- Pierre Roux, 'Innocuous Double Rounding of Basic Arithmetic Operations', Journal of Formalized Reasoning 7(1):131–142, 2014, DOI 10.6092/issn.1972-5787/4359. Coq proofs of the Figueroa bounds without underflow (Table I: +, −: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 1; ×, /: p2 ≥ 2p1; √: p2 ≥ 2p1 + 2), with optimality remarks. Table II adds conditions on emin under gradual underflow (e.g. ×: emin2 ≤ 2·emin1). Notation: Roux works in Flocq's FLT formats, where emin is the exponent of the least significant digit of the smallest subnormal (Flocq FLT.v: 'emin <= Fexp f' with an integer significand); in this package that is −qneg, not the IEEE normal minimum exponent 1 − bias. So Roux's 'emin2 ≤ 2·emin1' corresponds to 2·qneg f ≤ qneg g. Overflows are harmless whenever the narrow format is included in the wide one.
- Sylvie Boldo, Jacques-Henri Jourdan, Xavier Leroy, Guillaume Melquiond, 'Verified Compilation of Floating-Point Computations', Journal of Automated Reasoning 54(2):135–163, 2015, DOI 10.1007/s10817-014-9317-x. Mechanized IEEE 754 semantics in CompCert (Coq/Flocq) with strict, value-preserving floating-point optimizations.
- Sylvie Boldo and Guillaume Melquiond, 'Flocq: A Unified Library for Proving Floating-Point Algorithms in Coq', 2011 IEEE 20th Symposium on Computer Arithmetic (ARITH), pp. 243–252, DOI 10.1109/ARITH.2011.40 (a general mechanized formalization of floating-point rounding; bibliographic data verified, specific theorems not checked).
- Mechanized IEEE 754 models (none of which this package depends on): John Harrison, 'A Machine-Checked Theory of Floating Point Arithmetic', TPHOLs 1999, LNCS 1690, pp. 113–130, DOI 10.1007/3-540-48256-3_9; Lei Yu, 'A Formal Model of IEEE Floating Point Arithmetic', Archive of Formal Proofs, July 2013 (entry IEEE_Floating_Point); Lean 4 v4.34.1 core Init.Data.Float.Model (a parametric model with structure Format { mantissaBitsWithoutImplicit, exponentBits, he : 2 ≤ exponentBits } backing Float and Float32; inspected in the pinned toolchain source); FloatLib (R. J. George, W. Adkisson, A. Anandkumar, 'FloatLib: Verified Floating-Point Arithmetic in Lean', arXiv:2609.19352, 2026); FloatSpec (github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/FloatSpec, a Lean 4 port of the Coq Flocq library). No bridge between Toy.Float.FP and any of these is proved.
- David Menendez, Santosh Nagarakatte, Aarti Gupta, 'Alive-FP: Automated Verification of Floating Point Based Peephole Optimizations in LLVM', Static Analysis (SAS 2016), LNCS 9837, pp. 317–337, Springer, 2016, DOI 10.1007/978-3-662-53413-7_16. Abstract: it 'handles a class of floating point optimizations and fast-math optimizations involving signed zeros, not-a-number, and infinities, which do not result in loss of accuracy' and found 'seven wrong optimizations in LLVM' (SMT-based checks at fixed bit widths; the paper body was not accessible, so the attribution of the flag names nnan/ninf/nsz to it rests on its public implementation).
- Andres Nötzli, Fraser Brown, 'LifeJacket: Verifying Precise Floating-Point Optimizations in LLVM', Proceedings of the 5th ACM SIGPLAN International Workshop on State Of the Art in Program Analysis (SOAP 2016), pp. 24–29, DOI 10.1145/2931021.2931024 (arXiv:1603.09290). Verifies 43 LLVM floating-point peephole optimizations with SMT and finds eight incorrect ones; 'LifeJacket currently supports three of the five fast-math flags that LLVM implements: nnan, ninf, and nsz'; its Fig. 1 example +0.0 - (-0.0 - x) => x 'is not valid if x = −0.0'.
- Heiko Becker, Eva Darulova, Magnus O. Myreen, Zachary Tatlock, 'Icing: Supporting Fast-Math Style Optimizations in a Verified Compiler', Computer Aided Verification (CAV 2019), LNCS 11562, pp. 155–173, Springer, 2019, DOI 10.1007/978-3-030-25543-5_10. Formalized in HOL4 and connected to CakeML; optimizations such as fusing x * y + z into fma (its Rewrite 1) and associativity/commutativity (Rewrites 2–5) are allowed rewrites 'applied nondeterministically' in the semantics — the closest mechanized analogue of the permission model Expr.variants/ValidMod used here.
- LLVM Language Reference Manual, llvm/docs/LangRef.md at llvm/llvm-project commit a2f1d1aeb6f5c700ac9a15592b6f81c3ec41e6c6, section 'Fast-Math Flags' (lines 4466–4566).
  - nnan: 'Allow optimizations to assume the arguments and result are not NaN. If an argument is a nan, or the result would be a nan, it produces a poison value instead.'
  - ninf: the same for +/-Inf.
  - nsz: 'Unless otherwise mentioned, the sign bit of 0.0 or -0.0 input operands can be non-deterministically flipped.' The older wording, still in LLVM 20.1.0's LangRef.rst, was 'treat the sign of a zero argument or zero result as insignificant'.
  - contract: 'Allow floating-point contraction (e.g., fusing a multiply followed by an addition into a fused multiply-and-add)'.
  - reassoc: 'Allow algebraically equivalent transformations for floating-point instructions such as reassociation transformations. This may dramatically change results in floating-point.'
- llvm/lib/Analysis/InstructionSimplify.cpp at llvm/llvm-project commit ef72a0e441f65f73b7cf732bf05b8bcc1a8cf609. 'fsub nnan x, x ==> 0.0' (lines 6244–6247, inside a default-floating-point-environment guard). 'X * 0.0 --> 0.0 (with nnan and nsz)' (lines 6286–6289). 'fadd X, -0 ==> X' (lines 6144–6153, guarded so that it is not applied when the rounding mode can be TowardNegative unless nsz is present, or when signaling NaNs cannot be ignored). 'fadd X, 0 ==> X, when we know X is not -0' (lines 6155–6159; requires nsz or X ≠ −0).
- ISO/IEC 9899:201x Committee Draft N1570 (12 April 2011), HTML rendering of the committee draft.
  - §6.5 ¶8: 'A floating expression may be contracted, that is, evaluated as though it were a single operation, thereby omitting rounding errors implied by the source code'.
  - §7.12.2 ¶2: the FP_CONTRACT pragma allows or disallows contraction, with implementation-defined default.
  - Annex F.7 ¶1: a contracted expression is correctly rounded once.
  - Annex F.9.2 'Expression transformations'. 'x - x → 0.0 The expressions x - x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN or infinite'. '0 * x → 0.0 The expressions 0*x and 0.0 are not equivalent if x is a NaN, infinite, or -0' (this lists sufficient reasons; every negative finite x also gives −0). 'x + 0 → x The expressions x + 0 and x are not equivalent if x is -0, because (-0) + (+0) yields +0 (in the default rounding direction), not -0'. '-x ↔ 0 - x The expressions -x and 0 - x are not equivalent if x is +0, because -(+0) yields -0, but 0 - (+0) yields +0 (unless rounding is downward)'.
  - Annex F.9.3 'Relational operators' (the same text is F.8.3 in C99 N1256). 'x != x → false The expression x != x is true if x is a NaN.' 'x == x → true The expression x = x [sic; C99 N1256 F.8.3 reads 'The statement x == x'] is false if x is a NaN.' 'The sense of relational operators shall be maintained. This includes handling unordered cases as expressed by the source code.' EXAMPLE: 'if (a < b) f(); else g();' is not equivalent to 'if (a >= b) g(); else f();' (and the comments note that both raise 'invalid' if a and b are unordered) but is equivalent to 'if (!(a < b)) g(); else f();'.
- Rust RFC 3514 'float_semantics' (rust-lang/rfcs pull request #3514, merged 2024-07-27), text/3514-float-semantics.md at rust-lang/rfcs commit f17e8623ee2e2854570dcdb936a9f4ab08c0fcd4: strict IEEE semantics 'precludes many transformations, such as turning a*b + c into FMA operations', with the x*x - x*x versus x.mul_add(x, -x*x) example.
- Rust core library at rust-lang/rust commit 268bdaabaceb7688b974bae0f96b9c5c1b6ab991.
  - library/core/src/primitive_docs.rs (lines 1194–1207): primitive operations round with roundTiesToEven, and 'If the result of a sum exactly equals zero, the outcome is +0.0 unless both arguments were negative, then it is -0.0. Subtraction a - b is regarded as a sum a + (-b).'
  - library/core/src/num/f32.rs, f32::min (lines 838–861): 'If exactly one of the arguments is NaN (quiet or signaling), then the other argument is returned ... If the inputs compare equal (such as for the case of +0.0 and -0.0), either input may be returned non-deterministically'.
  - library/core/src/num/f32.rs, f32::minimum (lines 893–917; unstable, feature float_minimum_maximum): 'propagating NaN ... -0.0 is considered to be less than +0.0 ... This follows the IEEE 754-2019 semantics for minimum'.

### Observation dates

The kernel check and the axiom and hash audit recorded above were run on 2026-11-21, and the Lean toolchain tag commit was fetched from the GitHub API on 2026-11-21. The bibliographic and source checks recorded in the prior-art entries were completed by 2026-11-21.

## Revalidation

Requirements: `elan` (so that `lake` selects the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain`, `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`) and `python3`. No network access is needed after the toolchain is installed; there are no Lake dependencies.

**Primary check.** From the package directory:

```sh
python3 support/revalidate.py .
```

`support/revalidate.py` never modifies the package: it copies `lean/` to a temporary directory and

1. runs `lake build` (the library `Toy`);
2. rejects any Lean source containing `sorry`, `admit`, `native_decide`, or an `axiom` declaration;
3. computes, with Lean's `collectAxioms`, the axioms of every declaration of the library and fails if any is outside `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`;
4. for every Lean name referenced by `results.json` (fields `lean`, `lean_names`, `nonvacuity_witness_lean`, `positive_control_lean`, and hypothesis `counterexample`s) and every name recorded in `audit/statements.json`, recomputes the SHA-256 of the statement (the Lean `repr` of the declaration's type) and of its definition closure (for every package-local constant reachable from the statement: its name, the `repr` of its type, and, for non-theorems, the `repr` of its value; theorem bodies excluded), and compares both with `audit/statements.json`.

It exits 0 if and only if all checks pass.

**Manual axiom listing.** From `lean/`:

```sh
lake build
lake env lean RevalidationAudit.lean
```

`RevalidationAudit.lean` is outside the `Toy` library target. It prints `#print axioms` output for each of the 241 declarations listed under **Evidence**; each line must report no axioms or a subset of `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`.

**Cheaper targeted checks.** To recheck a single result after a change, build the library and run `#print axioms Toy.Float.<name>` for that result, its counterexamples and its non-vacuity witness; compare the statement text with the quote under **Findings**.

**What a passing check establishes.** It establishes that the theorems quoted under **Findings** are kernel-checked under the pinned toolchain with the recorded axioms and that their statements and definition closures are the recorded ones. It does not check the prose, the modelling bridges or the citations.
